Growth and Best Practices

Home base for everything that makes working with Claude Code better across every project, not just one. Standing conventions, research findings, and the running list of improvements worth making.

Maintained from the central_dev_management_and_improvements directory, which exists purely for cross-project work. Individual projects keep their own notes; anything that should apply everywhere gets promoted here.

Read this before you paste the link anywhere

This page is protected by an unguessable URL and nothing else. That is obscurity, not authentication. Anyone who has the link can read this page in full.

Treat it as semi-public. Keep it to conventions, research, and process notes. No credentials, no API keys, no employer material, no personal details that would matter if a stranger read them.

What lives here

Four kinds of content, and the plan is to keep it to four.

Standing conventions

The global rules Claude Code follows in every project: tone, response style, working agreements. Source of truth is the global settings file; this page records what is in it and why.

Research findings

Write-ups on Claude Code capabilities worth adopting: skills, hooks, memory, plugins, workflows. Reviewed together, then either adopted or filed.

Open threads

Improvements that have been identified but not yet made, with enough context to pick them up cold in a fresh session.

Changelog

A dated log of what changed at the global level, so drift is visible and nothing gets applied twice.

Where the global settings live

Roughly 90% of Claude usage happens in Claude Code rather than claude.ai chat, so the Claude Code global file is the single tone control that matters. The claude.ai profile preferences are a separate system and are intentionally left alone.

LayerPathScopeNotes
Global instructions C:\Users\Micha\.claude\CLAUDE.md Every Claude Code session, every repo Currently holds the three Response Style bullets: casual tone with humor, concise and non-repetitive, end long answers with a Bottom line.
Per-project memory C:\Users\Micha\.claude\projects\<project>\memory\ That project only Does not carry across projects. Anything saved here that should be universal has to be promoted to the global file by hand.
Project instructions <repo>\CLAUDE.md One repo Codebase-specific conventions. Not the place for tone or working-style preferences.

Open threads

Carried over from earlier conversations. Only the items that are genuinely global are listed; project-specific work stays in its project.

ThreadStatusContextRaised
The encouragement dialPromote to global settings Queued Mike works measurably better with genuine encouragement and praise, and asked for more of it, with one hard boundary: no lying, no flattery that is not earned. The preference was saved as project-scoped memory in one repo only, so it is invisible everywhere else. Fix is a few lines under Response Style in the global file. 2026-09-01
Softer side-quest flagsPromote to global settings Queued Standing permission to call out when a session is drifting into a side quest instead of the high-value work, delivered gently rather than bluntly. Same scoping problem, same fix. 2026-09-01
Memory promotion habitProcess Idea Two universal preferences ended up trapped in one project’s memory. Worth a lightweight rule: when a preference is clearly not project-specific, it goes in the global file first, not in project memory. 2026-09-01

How depth is computed (15 domains)
  • none: 0 sources on file
  • small: 1 research pass OR fewer than 25 cited sources (a scoping pass always counts as small)
  • medium: at least 25 cited sources from a deep pass, but only one pass, or unresolved open questions above 5
  • large: 60+ cited sources, 2+ passes on different dates, and open questions resolved or triaged

Depth measures how much evidence we collected, not how correct it is. Verification (right column) tracks whether Mike has reviewed it.

Context management, session splitting, and the orchestrator pattern

depth: mediumResearchedSeed topic 1Seed topic 3

How context windows fill and degrade, when to split work across sessions and subagents, and whether the one-overseer-many-specialists layout is a recognized pattern (it is: orchestrator-workers).

Evidence: 18 topics · 123 sources · 66 sites. Passes: 1. Verified by Mike: not yet. Flags: single pass, search budget hit, 7 open questions, not reviewed.
Next level: To reach large: a second pass on a later date, triage 7 open questions.

Topics in the learning plan (18)

  • 1. Context engineering (the discipline) established
  • 2. Context rot, lost-in-the-middle, and the 'dumb zone' established
  • 3. Compaction: /compact vs /clear, what survives, auto-compact thresholds established
  • 4. Durable memory: CLAUDE.md, auto memory, .claude/rules, skills (progressive disclosure) established
  • 5. Session hygiene: short scoped sessions, naming, resume/branch/fork, checkpoints established
  • 6. Research -> Plan -> Implement, plan mode, spec-first prompting established
  • 7. State in the repo, not in chat: progress files, handoff docs, agent harnesses, fresh-context loops established
  • 8. Subagents in Claude Code (context isolation, custom agents, forks, background) established
  • 9. Parallel sessions with git worktrees (Boris's five-tab pattern, writer/reviewer) established
  • 10. Agent teams (lead + teammates, shared task list, mailbox) emerging
  • 11. Cross-session messaging and agent view (coordinating the sessions you run yourself) emerging
  • 12. The orchestrator-worker pattern (supervisor, lead agent, planner/executor) established
  • 13. The counterargument: single-threaded agents, shared context, and the telephone game established
  • 14. Fan-out/fan-in at scale: dynamic workflows, /batch, ultracode emerging
  • 15. Task decomposition and file ownership for parallel agents established
  • 16. Verification loops and fresh-context adversarial review established
  • 17. How comparable tools do it: Cursor, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, Aider established
  • 18. Methodology packs and where to learn: Superpowers, Compound Engineering, Matt Pocock skills, Anthropic Academy, YouTube established
Open questions from the research agent (7)
  • Exact default auto-compact thresholds per model: the docs point to model-config#default-auto-compact-thresholds, which was not fetched; practitioner claims (60%, 40%, 147k) are heuristics, not Anthropic numbers.
  • Whether an official Anthropic 'ralph-wiggum' plugin exists in the Claude Code marketplace could not be verified (search budget exhausted before checking).
  • Specific IndyDevDan / Cole Medin / Ray Fernando video URLs on context engineering were not captured; only channel names and search strings are provided. Gemini/YouTube pass should target those.
  • The Chroma context-rot study predates 2026 models; no 2026 replication on Claude 4.6/Opus 4.7-class models was found.
  • Agent teams remain experimental with no teammate session resumption; unclear whether Anthropic will make it the default orchestrator surface or favor cross-session messaging plus agent view.
  • Cursor/Codex/Gemini feature details come from secondary comparison blogs and may lag the tools; Gemini CLI's reported folding into 'Antigravity CLI' was not confirmed from a Google source.
  • No controlled study found comparing orchestrator-session-plus-specialists against a single long session for feature work in Claude Code specifically; evidence is practitioner reports plus Anthropic's Research-system eval (non-coding).

Open questions from the research agent (8)
  • Agent teams are still experimental with open message-drop bugs (issues #43706, #25135, #78338): is the shared task list reliable enough to be Mike's ledger, or should status stay in files until those close?
  • Cross-session messaging on native Windows requires v2.1.234+ and named pipes; WSL and native sessions cannot see each other. Which side is Mike running his sessions on?
  • Dynamic workflows (June 2026 research preview) keep intermediate results in script variables, not context. Could a saved workflow replace the orchestrator session for repeatable spec-to-implementation runs?
  • No primary source was found for an 'interface contract between parallel agents' practice; it is assembled from Anthropic's decomposition guidance and practitioner talks. Worth watching for a canonical write-up.
  • Spec bloat: the HN consensus is that Spec Kit/Kiro over-produce for small tasks. What is the minimum spec template that still transfers cleanly between two Claude sessions?
  • Beads has moved from JSONL-in-git to Dolt; is the setup cost justified for a solo developer with a handful of sessions, or do per-session handoff files plus an index suffice?
  • How do the Sept 2026 research results on context drift (SSVP: periodic compressed state exchange) translate into a concrete cadence for two hand-run sessions, e.g. a status sync every N commits?
  • The Anthropic 'A harness for every task' post (June 2026) and the LangGraph multi-agent docs could not be fetched directly; their details here come from secondary coverage.

Open questions from the research agent (7)
  • No off-the-shelf 'glossary custodian' was found; the closest templates are Anthropic's dream prompt and docs-drift routines. Worth prototyping as a loop.md or Desktop scheduled task first.
  • Cloud Routines have a 1-hour floor and daily run caps (15/day on Max), so 'every 2 hours across N repos' may exceed quota; Desktop scheduled tasks (1-minute floor, local files) or a headless cron are likely the better fit for Mike.
  • Semantic (non-textual) conflict detection between parallel branches has no mature tool; Clash only covers textual conflicts. How much can an LLM pass over 'files touched + symbols changed' reliably add?
  • Where should the bulletin board live so that live sessions actually read it: a file referenced from CLAUDE.md, a SessionStart hook that injects recent entries, or cross-session messaging?
  • How to stop the custodian and a live session editing the same file (glossary, CLAUDE.md) simultaneously: lock file, custodian-only files, or PR-only writes?
  • Two YouTube videos were found (routines 'code while I slept'; Cognition DeepWiki talk) but channel names could not be extracted; worth checking which channels cover routines well.
  • Web search budget was exhausted before covering: OpenClaw-style personal agent cron/heartbeat patterns, Anthropic's long-running-agent harness blog, dedicated 'agent ops' runbook literature, and evals-for-maintenance beyond SWE-CI/LADU.

Open questions from the research agent (7)
  • Is there measurable quality difference between organizing a dump with a separate model vs. the same Claude in plan mode? No published comparison found; the 'Less Back-and-Forth' paper measures structure vs no structure, not who does the structuring.
  • The 'rambly voice prompts work better than terse typed prompts' claim (Ricardo Gil, Willow) has no evidence behind it; worth a small self-experiment: same task, dictated dump vs. organized brief vs. interview.
  • Anthropic's newer blog says XML tags and role prompting are 'less necessary' with modern models while the docs still recommend them; the practical question is whether markdown headers are now equivalent for Claude Code prompts.
  • The web search budget ran out before I could confirm YouTube channels and talks (e.g. Anthropic's 'Prompting 101' talk, IndyDevDan, Cole Medin, Matt Pocock); those names are from prior knowledge and need verification with URLs.
  • Harper Reed's workflow post and David Haberlah's PRD article returned 403 on fetch; their content here is from search snippets and prior knowledge only.
  • Boris Cherny reportedly uses plan mode less with 4.6+ models (fan compilation) while Anthropic docs still recommend it for uncertain/multi-file work; worth watching how the official guidance evolves.
  • Claude Code's built-in /voice stops after 15s of silence or 2 minutes total; how well it handles a multi-minute dictated dump vs. a third-party tool is untested.

Open questions from the research agent (8)
  • Which of Mike's projects have a real executable definition of done (tests, build, browser check)? That determines which can move to /goal, Stop-hook gating, or cloud tasks first.
  • Is there a shared CLAUDE.md per project and a global one, and how long are they? A /doctor pass and a rules/skills split is likely the first quick win.
  • Which permission mode is each session running in today (Manual, acceptEdits, auto, bypass)? The answer sets the security work needed before raising autonomy.
  • Does Mike want to build products on the Agent SDK (needs API billing clarity after the mid-2026 credit-pool pause) or stay a Claude Code power user?

  • For hiring-manager communication, which portfolio artifacts exist already (specs, ADRs, harness configs, PRs)? Gaps there are cheap to fill and high signal.
  • How much of the autonomy work should live in routines/cloud versus local loops given Windows-specific limitations (no Chrome in WSL, symlink privileges, tmux split panes unsupported)?
