19Orchestrator-worker delegation and the 'task brief'

establishedSeed topic 1
Search fororchestrator-workers patternlead agent / subagenttask decompositiondelegation promptobjective + output format + tools + boundarieshierarchical delegationcapability narrowing

What it is. The dominant multi-agent shape: one lead plans, spawns workers with scoped briefs, and synthesises. Anthropic's Research system found the brief is the whole interface: every worker needs an objective, an output format, guidance on tools/sources, and explicit task boundaries, or workers duplicate each other's searches. Workers return a condensed summary (roughly 1,000-2,000 tokens) rather than raw context.

Why it matters for you. Mike's orchestrator-plus-specialists layout is literally this pattern; the quality of the brief he sends each specialist session determines whether the spec that comes back is usable.

Best practices (5)
  • Every brief states: goal, deliverable format, files/areas in scope, files/areas out of scope, and the verification the worker must run before reporting.
  • Decompose along context boundaries, not by type of work (Anthropic, Jan 2026): a worker should need minimal transfer of the lead's history.
  • Start with 3-5 workers; more increases coordination overhead faster than throughput.
  • Have workers write large outputs to files and return only a summary plus paths, to avoid copying big payloads through the orchestrator.
  • Keep a human-in-the-loop checkpoint at handoff boundaries when work is irreversible.
Where to learn (5 sources)
  • How we built our multi-agent research system Anthropic Engineering, 2025-06, blog
  • When to use multi-agent systems (and when not to) Anthropic, 2026-01, blog
  • Building effective agents Anthropic Engineering, 2024-12, blog
  • Best practices for Claude Code subagents PubNub, blog
  • Best Practices for Multi-Agent Orchestration with Claude (discussion #1313) anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python, thread

Names to know: Anthropic Applied AI team, Erik Schluntz (co-author of Building effective agents)

20Claude Code's parallel-agent surfaces and how to pick one

emergingSeed topic 2
Search forsubagentsfork / /subtaskagent teamscross-session messagingagent view / background sessionsdynamic workflows / ultracode/batchroutines

What it is. Claude Code now ships six distinct ways to run multiple Claudes, each with a different coordination model: subagents (results return to caller), forks (inherit full history), agent teams (lead + teammates with mailbox and shared task list), cross-session messaging (independent sessions you run yourself pass plain-text messages), agent view (background sessions, results only to you), and dynamic workflows (a JS script holds the plan; results live in script variables, not context). The docs' decision axes are: who coordinates, do workers need to talk, and do they touch the same files.

Why it matters for you. Mike's setup is 'independent sessions you run yourself', which the docs explicitly map to worktrees + cross-session messaging, not agent teams. Knowing the vocabulary lets him read the right doc page instead of improvising.

Best practices (3)
  • Use subagents when only the result matters; forks when a worker needs the parent's history; teams when workers must debate; workflows when a job outgrows a handful of subagents.
  • Keep Mike-style hand-run sessions as the default: lower token cost, resumable, and the lead never loses teammates on /resume (a known agent-teams limitation).
  • Check the 'Run agents in parallel' comparison page first when unsure; it is the canonical decision table.
Where to learn (6 sources)
  • Run agents in parallel Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Create custom subagents Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Orchestrate subagents at scale with dynamic workflows Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Manage multiple agents with agent view Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Claude Code Adds Dynamic Workflows for Parallel Agent Coordination InfoQ, 2026-06, blog
  • Claude Code Multi-Agent Orchestration: 6 Patterns (2026) The Prompt Shelf, 2026, blog

Names to know: Anthropic Claude Code team

21File-based handoff and progress files (HANDOFF.md, PROGRESS.md, planning-with-files)

emergingSeed topic 2
Search forHANDOFF.md patternsession handofftask_plan.md / findings.md / progress.mdPLAN.mdcontext window = RAM, filesystem = diskper-turn re-injectionappend-only handoffsINDEX.md

What it is. A family of conventions where the durable state of work lives in markdown on disk: what was done, what is pending, what failed and why, key decisions, and the single next action. Tools like planning-with-files re-inject the plan every turn via SessionStart/UserPromptSubmit/PreCompact hooks and gate completion on the plan. The multi-session variant gives each session its own timestamped handoff file plus an append-only index, because a single shared file is last-writer-wins.

Why it matters for you. This is mechanism (b), and the community has already hit its failure mode (one shared file, last writer wins, stale entries) and standardised the fix: per-session files, append-only, one index, plus a re-read on session start.

Best practices (5)
  • One handoff file per session/feature, named YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM_<session>.md, never edited by another session; a small INDEX.md that only grows.
  • Mandatory sections: Goal, Done (with absolute paths), Did NOT work (with errors), Current state, Key decisions, Next step, Closure audit.
  • Write atomically at end of session or before compaction (PreCompact hook); read and summarise in 3-5 lines at start (SessionStart hook).
  • Keep the always-loaded layer tiny: CLAUDE.md (conventions) + current HANDOFF (state); everything else on demand.
  • Mark abandoned work ABANDONED rather than deleting another session's file.
Where to learn (6 sources)
  • The HANDOFF.md Pattern - How to Keep Claude Code Productive Across Sessions Matthew Diakonov (Fazm), 2026-03, blog
  • planning-with-files (repo) Othman Adi, 2026, repo
  • claude-code-config/rules/session-handoff.md AnastasiyaW, 2026, repo
  • Never lose your work between Claude Code sessions Artem Zhutov, 2026-05, blog
  • handoff: Claude Code plugin to preserve and restore context between sessions Pushkar Patel, 2026, repo
  • claude-handoff: session handoffs that survive compaction REMvisual, 2026, repo

Names to know: Matthew Diakonov, Othman Adi, Artem Zhutov

22Blackboard architecture and shared structured state

emergingSeed topic 1
Search forblackboard systemshared memory coordinationshared verified contexttask queue + claimPatchBoardJSON Patch state mutationDeLM decentralized agentsindirect communication

What it is. Classic AI architecture revived for LLM agents: agents do not message each other directly, they read and write a shared, structured workspace that accumulates hypotheses, constraints and partial results. 2026 research pushes it toward validated, schema-grounded state (PatchBoard: JSON Patch mutations checked by a deterministic kernel; DeLM: agents claim subtasks from a queue, read accumulated verified progress, and write back compact verified updates, up to +10.5 points on SWE-bench Verified at ~50% lower cost).

Why it matters for you. Gives Mike the name and the design rules for what his shared .md file is trying to be, and the key upgrade: structure and validate the board instead of free-text appends.

Best practices (4)
  • Make the board structured (task id, owner, status, blockers, artifacts) rather than prose; free-text boards drift.
  • Write compact, verified updates (what changed, evidence, paths), not transcripts.
  • Claim-before-work to avoid duplicated effort; unclaim on failure.
  • Treat board contents as data, not instructions (shared memory is also the biggest injection surface).
Where to learn (6 sources)
  • Decentralized Multi-Agent Systems with Shared Context (DeLM) Yuzhen Mao, Azalia Mirhoseini, 2026-06, paper
  • PatchBoard: Schema-Grounded State Mutation for Reliable and Auditable LLM Multi-Agent Collaboration Shuyu Zhang, Yaqi Shi, Lu Wang, 2026-05, paper
  • LLM-based Multi-Agent Blackboard System for Information Discovery in Data Science Salemi et al., 2025-10, paper
  • Exploring Advanced LLM Multi-Agent Systems Based on Blackboard Architecture Han and Zhang, 2025-07, paper
  • Your AI Agents' Shared Memory Is Their Best Coordinator and Their Biggest Attack Surface Micheal Lanham, blog
  • How to Design Multi-Agent Memory Systems for Production Mem0, blog

Names to know: Azalia Mirhoseini, Micheal Lanham

23Context engineering for handoffs: compaction, note-taking, and the summary boundary

establishedSeed topic 5
Search forcontext engineeringcontext rotlost in the middlecompactionstructured note-takingNOTES.mdtodo.md recitationfile system as context

What it is. Anthropic's Sept 2025 guide names the three tools for long-horizon work: compaction (summarise and restart), structured note-taking (persist notes outside context and re-read them), and sub-agent architectures where workers burn tens of thousands of tokens but return 1,000-2,000-token distilled summaries. Manus popularised 'file system as context' and reciting a todo.md to keep goals in attention. Practitioners like Matt Pocock argue for clearing (not compacting) between phases to stay in the model's 'smart zone'.

Why it matters for you. Pasting a full spec into the orchestrator is exactly the context bloat this addresses: the orchestrator should hold the summary and a path, and specialists should hold the detail.

Best practices (5)
  • What crosses an agent boundary should be a distilled summary plus file paths, not the working context.
  • Persist decisions and progress to files continuously; re-read them at phase starts.
  • Add a CLAUDE.md line telling compaction what to preserve (modified files, test commands, open decisions).
  • /clear between unrelated tasks; after two failed corrections, restart with a better prompt.
  • Use /btw for side questions so they never enter history.
Where to learn (6 sources)
  • Effective context engineering for AI agents Anthropic Engineering, 2025-09, blog
  • Context engineering: memory, compaction, and tool clearing (cookbook) Anthropic, docs
  • Context Engineering for agents LangChain, 2025, blog
  • Two approaches to context engineering: Manus vs Context Space Context Space (dev.to), blog
  • Full Walkthrough: Workflow for AI Coding - Matt Pocock AI Engineer (YouTube), 2026-04, video
  • How Claude remembers your project (memory, compaction survival) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs

Names to know: Matt Pocock, Manus (Yichao Ji's context-engineering post), Lance Martin (LangChain)

24Spec-driven development and what makes a spec transferable

emergingSeed topic 4
Search forSDDspec-driven developmentGitHub Spec KitKiro specsTessl spec-as-sourceconstitution / specify / clarify / plan / tasks / implementrequirements.md / design.md / tasks.mdEARS acceptance criteria

What it is. The 2025-26 movement to make a written spec the primary artifact that agents implement from. Spec Kit (v1.0, 30+ agents) runs constitution -> specify -> clarify -> plan -> tasks -> implement -> converge with artifacts under specs/ and .specify/; Kiro generates requirements.md, design.md, tasks.md; Tessl goes further to spec-as-source. A transferable spec has: summary, user stories, testable acceptance criteria, functional and non-functional requirements, explicit edge cases and error handling, non-goals, named files/interfaces, and an end-to-end verification step. Claude Code's own docs recommend having Claude interview you, write SPEC.md, then execute in a fresh session.

Why it matters for you. This is the direct answer to 'how should a specialist's spec sheet be written so the orchestrator (or another session) can act on it without a Q&A round trip'.

Best practices (12)
  • Spec says what and why; the plan says how. Keep tech choices out of the spec unless the constitution mandates them.
  • Acceptance criteria as observable facts ('API responds within 200 ms'), ideally EARS-style; they are the definition of done.
  • Name in-scope files and interfaces, state non-goals, and end with a verification step the implementer must run.
  • Run a clarify pass to surface [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] items before handing off; iterate until no questions remain.
  • Keep specs under ~300 lines; split large features into several specs.
  • Target spec-anchored, not spec-as-source: HN practitioners report ceremony bloat (16 acceptance criteria for a small bug) and stale specs when there is no reconciliation step.
  • Version the spec in the repo next to the code and reference it by path (@specs/foo.md) instead of pasting it.
  • Make specs self-contained: files and interfaces involved, explicit out-of-scope, end-to-end verification step.
  • Keep a project 'constitution' (non-negotiable principles) that every spec inherits.
  • Turn each spec into a task list with testable completion states; that list is what an unattended loop consumes.
  • Version specs in the repo and update them when the code changes; a stale spec is worse than none for agents.
  • Try Spec Kit with Claude Code before adopting a heavier framework; keep what sticks.
Where to learn (12 sources)
  • How to write a good spec for AI agents Addy Osmani, 2026-01, blog
  • Spec Kit (repo) GitHub, 2026, repo
  • Write an effective spec.md file (Spec Kit training module) Microsoft Learn, 2026-01, docs
  • Kiro specs AWS Kiro, docs
  • A look at Spec Kit, GitHub's spec-driven software development toolkit Tessl, 2025, blog
  • Understanding Spec-Driven-Development: Kiro, Spec-Kit, and Tessl (HN thread) Hacker News, 2025-10, thread

Names to know: Addy Osmani, John Lam (Spec Kit research), Den Delimarsky (Spec Kit maintainer), Guy Podjarny (Tessl), Matt Pocock ('grill me' skill), GitHub Spec Kit maintainers, AWS Kiro team, BMAD-METHOD community

25Interface contracts and decomposition along ownership boundaries

emergingSeed topic 4
Search forcontract-firstinterface contract between agentsfile ownership partitioningdecompose by context boundarycross-layer coordinationdeep modules / minimal interfacesAPI contract before implementationsibling roster

What it is. When two sessions each own a layer (frontend/backend, API/DB), the handoff artifact is an agreed interface (types, endpoints, schemas, events) fixed before either side implements. Anthropic's guidance is to split work only where context can be truly isolated and to give each teammate a distinct set of files; agent teams docs call this cross-layer coordination and warn that two teammates editing one file overwrites each other. Matt Pocock frames the human's job as specifying deep modules with minimal interfaces and delegating the internals.

Why it matters for you. Mike's specialists own feature areas; the cheapest handoff between them is a contract file, which prevents both duplicated work and the integration surprises that force a second round of spec-passing.

Best practices (4)
  • Before parallel implementation, have the orchestrator produce a contract doc (or typed schema file) and get both specialists to acknowledge it.
  • Assign file/directory ownership explicitly in every brief; put the ownership map in the shared board.
  • Changes to a contract are messages worth sending (cross-session messaging's own example is 'schema migration finished, rebasing on main is safe').
  • Keep contracts small and typed where possible (OpenAPI, TS types, JSON schema) so they can be validated, not just read.
Where to learn (4 sources)
  • When to use multi-agent systems (and when not to) Anthropic, 2026-01, blog
  • Orchestrate teams of Claude Code sessions (Avoid file conflicts, Cross-layer coordination) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Full Walkthrough: Workflow for AI Coding - Matt Pocock (notes) shanraisshan / Matt Pocock, 2026-04, video
  • Message your other Claude Code sessions (Coordinate parallel worktrees) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs

Names to know: Matt Pocock, John Ousterhout (deep modules, via Pocock)

26Structured outputs and schema-validated handoffs

emergingSeed topic 5
Search forstructured outputoutputFormat json_schemaagent() schema in workflowshandoff input_typevalidate at every boundarystandardized communication protocolstyped handoff payloadreceipts

What it is. Replacing free-text handoffs with schema-checked ones. The Claude Agent SDK returns a validated structured_output when you pass a JSON schema; dynamic-workflow scripts call agent(prompt, {schema}) so each phase feeds typed data to the next; OpenAI's Agents SDK lets a handoff carry a typed input_type and an input_filter over history. MAST's structural fixes and Redis's practitioner write-up both land on 'validate LLM outputs with schema checks before passing downstream'.

Why it matters for you. For status updates and spec summaries between sessions, a tiny fixed schema (task, status, files, evidence, next) removes most telephone-game ambiguity even when the transport is a markdown file or a plain-text message.

Best practices (4)
  • Define one small status schema and require every session's update to conform to it (even in markdown front-matter).
  • For programmatic pipelines, use the Agent SDK outputFormat or workflow agent() schema so malformed handoffs fail loudly.
  • Keep human-readable prose alongside the structured block; the schema is for the receiver, the prose for you.
  • Validate before you forward: the orchestrator should check a specialist's output against the spec's acceptance criteria before relaying it.
Where to learn (5 sources)
  • Get structured output from agents (Agent SDK) Claude Code Docs, 2026, docs
  • Orchestrate subagents at scale with dynamic workflows (agent() schema) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • OpenAI Agents SDK: Handoffs OpenAI, docs
  • Why multi-agent LLM systems fail & how to fix them Jim Allen Wallace (Redis), 2026-04, blog
  • Structured Outputs in the Claude Agent SDK Team 400, 2026-04, blog

Names to know: Jim Allen Wallace (Redis)

27Failure modes: MAST taxonomy, telephone-game drift, context drift, stale state

establishedSeed topic 5
Search forMASTWhy Do Multi-Agent LLM Systems Failinter-agent misalignmenttask derailmentinformation withholdingreasoning-action mismatchtelephone gamecascading context degradation

What it is. The research vocabulary for what goes wrong. MAST (Cemri et al., Berkeley) annotated 1,600+ traces into 14 failure modes: system design (43.8%: disobeying spec, step repetition, missing termination), inter-agent misalignment (32.15%: conversation reset, failing to ask for clarification, task derailment, information withholding, ignoring another agent's input, reasoning-action mismatch), and task verification (23.5%). 2026 papers add context drift between concurrent agents (fixed by periodically exchanging compressed state summaries) and behavioural drift over long interactions; practitioners add conformity bias and stale shared state.

Why it matters for you. Each of Mike's three mechanisms has a named failure mode: copy-paste is lossy handoff (telephone), the shared file is stale-state and last-writer-wins, and messages hit information withholding or silent drops. Naming them makes the mitigation searchable.

Best practices (5)
  • Tactical fixes from MAST: precise instructions and role specs, re-engagement protocols on inconsistency, self-verification steps, defined conversation patterns.
  • Periodic state sync: have sessions exchange compressed state summaries at phase boundaries (the SSVP idea) rather than replaying transcripts.
  • Use a different context (fresh subagent or another session) for verification to dodge monoculture/conformity bias.
  • Put correlation ids (task id, spec version) on every handoff so you can trace where drift entered.
  • Explicit termination conditions and claimed-task ownership to kill duplicated work.
Where to learn (7 sources)
  • Why Do Multi-Agent LLM Systems Fail? (MAST) Mert Cemri et al. (UC Berkeley), 2025-03, paper
  • Hallucination as Context Drift: Synchronization Protocols for Multi-Agent LLM Systems Carson Rodrigues, 2026-06, paper
  • Agent Drift: Quantifying Behavioral Degradation in Multi-Agent LLM Systems Over Extended Interactions Abhishek Rath, 2026-01, paper
  • Why multi-agent LLM systems fail & how to fix them Jim Allen Wallace (Redis), 2026-04, blog
  • Why do multi agent LLM systems fail (and how to fix) - 2026 Guide Future AGI, 2026, blog
  • Are Your Multi-Agent Systems Failing for These 7 Reasons? Galileo, blog

Names to know: Mert Cemri, Matei Zaharia, Ion Stoica, Joseph Gonzalez, Carson Rodrigues, Abhishek Rath

28Isolation and merge: worktrees, file leases, merge queues

establishedSeed topic 5
Search forgit worktreesclaude --worktreeEnterWorktreeisolation: worktree.worktreeincludeworktree.baseRef headfile reservations / leasesmerge queue / Refinery

What it is. The mechanical answer to conflicting edits: each session or subagent works in its own git worktree (built into Claude Code since v2.1.49; agent view auto-isolates background sessions; subagents can set isolation: worktree), and Claude Code actively blocks edits or git commands that would reach the main checkout. Where worktrees are not used (agent teams), the alternatives are advisory file leases (MCP Agent Mail) or a serialised merge queue (Gas Town's Refinery, Multiclaude's ratchet).

Why it matters for you. If Mike's specialist sessions share one checkout, conflicting edits and stale files are guaranteed; worktrees plus messaging is the documented pairing for hand-run sessions.

Best practices (5)
  • One worktree per specialist session (claude --worktree <feature>); add .claude/worktrees/ to .gitignore and use .worktreeinclude for .env files.
  • Set worktree.baseRef to head when specialists must build on in-progress work.
  • Diff before merge (git diff main..branch), merge one branch at a time, keep active worktrees under ~6-10.
  • Announce landed changes to affected sessions via SendMessage.
  • If you cannot isolate, assign file ownership in writing and consider advisory leases.
Where to learn (5 sources)
  • Run parallel sessions with worktrees Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Git Worktrees + Claude Code: The 2026 Playbook for Running Parallel Agents Developers Digest, 2026, blog
  • Parallel Claude Code Sessions with Git Worktrees: The Production Setup Code With Seb, 2026, blog
  • MCP Agent Mail (file reservations / leases) Dicklesworthstone, 2025, repo
  • Multi-agent orchestration for Claude Code in 2026 (Agent Teams vs Gas Town vs Multiclaude) Shipyard, 2026-03, blog

Names to know: Dan Lorenc (Multiclaude), Steve Yegge (Refinery merge queue)

29Verification gates as the handoff acceptance test

emergingSeed topic 4
Search fordefinition of doneacceptance criteria/goalStop hookTaskCompleted hookSubagentStop hookadversarial review subagentevaluator-optimizer

What it is. A handoff is only complete when a check the receiver can run passes. Claude Code exposes this at several levels: verification criteria in the prompt; /goal (a fresh Haiku evaluator judges a condition after every turn); deterministic Stop or TaskCompleted hooks that block completion; and an adversarial reviewer subagent that sees only the diff and the plan. Anthropic's evaluator-optimizer workflow is the general form; the docs recommend a Writer session and a Reviewer session with fresh context.

Why it matters for you. This turns 'the specialist says it is done' into 'the spec's acceptance criteria passed', which is the only cure for premature or incorrect verification (23.5% of MAST failures).

Best practices (4)
  • Every spec ends with a verification step; every handoff includes the evidence (test output, command run, screenshot).
  • Use /goal <acceptance criteria> or a TaskCompleted hook so completion is judged by something other than the worker.
  • Review in a fresh context (subagent or second session) against PLAN.md/SPEC.md; tell the reviewer to report only gaps affecting correctness or stated requirements.
  • Prefer a different model or effort level for the verifier to avoid shared blind spots.
Where to learn (5 sources)
  • Keep Claude working toward a goal (/goal) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Best practices for Claude Code (Give Claude a way to verify its work; Add an adversarial review step) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Hooks reference (Stop, SubagentStop, TaskCompleted) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Building effective agents (evaluator-optimizer) Anthropic Engineering, 2024-12, blog
  • Claude Code Hooks: Complete Guide to All 30 Lifecycle Events claudefa.st, 2026, blog

Names to know: Anthropic Claude Code team

30Durable work ledgers: Beads, Gas Town, Agent Mail, Multiclaude

emergingSeed topic 1
Search forBeads (bd)git-backed issue tracker as agent memorybd ready / bd prime / --claimhash-based issue idsGas TownMayor / Polecats / Refinery / Witness / DeaconconvoysMCP Agent Mail

What it is. Steve Yegge's answer to session amnesia and handoffs: Beads is a dependency-aware issue graph stored in a version-controlled database (Dolt, JSONL export) so any agent can run bd ready to find unblocked work, atomically claim it, and bd prime to reload context; Gas Town (Jan 2026) layers roles on top (Mayor coordinates, Polecats work in isolated worktrees, Refinery serialises merges, Witness monitors). MCP Agent Mail adds email-like threaded messaging and file leases keyed to bead ids. Multiclaude takes the opposite bet: minimal coordination, merge whatever passes CI.

Why it matters for you. This is the mature version of 'all sessions deposit updates into a shared .md': a queryable, claimable ledger instead of prose, which fixes staleness, duplication and last-writer-wins at once. Also the most-discussed practitioner topic in this space in 2026.

Best practices (4)
  • Treat the ledger as the source of truth for status; treat chat and messages as ephemeral pointers into it.
  • Claim before work; record blockers as dependencies so ready-work is computed, not eyeballed.
  • Key messages/threads by task id for traceability.
  • Be sober about cost and complexity: Gas Town reports $100/hour burns and paddo.dev's take is that vanilla setups beat both BMAD-style role hierarchies and swarms for most developers.
Where to learn (9 sources)
  • Beads README Steve Yegge, 2026, repo
  • Introducing Beads: a coding agent memory system Steve Yegge, 2025-10, blog
  • Welcome to Gas Town Steve Yegge, 2026-01, blog
  • Gas Town (yegge.ai) Steve Yegge, 2026, docs
  • GasTown and the Two Kinds of Multi-Agent paddo.dev, 2026-01, blog
  • Gas Town: Running Dozens of Claude Code Instances at Once Justin Abrahms, 2026-01, blog

Names to know: Steve Yegge, Dan Lorenc, Dicklesworthstone, paddo.dev

31Autonomous loops with fresh context (handing off to your future self)

emerging
Search forRalph Wiggum loop/ralph-loopcompletion promise/loop/goalroutinesscheduled tasksfresh context per iteration

What it is. Geoffrey Huntley's Ralph loop re-runs the same prompt with a fresh context until a completion phrase appears, with all progress living in files and git history. Anthropic shipped it as an official plugin (Stop-hook based) and added supported equivalents: /goal (condition judged by a separate model), /loop (interval), and cloud routines (cron, API or GitHub triggers). The design lesson is that every iteration is a handoff between two agents that share only the filesystem.

Why it matters for you. Same handoff discipline Mike needs between sessions, in its purest form: if a loop can resume from files alone, so can a specialist session that was restarted.

Best practices (9)
  • Write the prompt/plan file with phased goals and explicit completion criteria; use --max-iterations as the safety net.
  • Keep the loop's state in a plan file plus git commits; never rely on the loop's context.
  • Prefer /goal or a Stop hook for in-session loops and routines for unattended scheduled work.
  • Good for well-defined tasks with automatic verification; bad for tasks needing human judgment.
  • Only loop on tasks with clear completion criteria and mechanical execution: refactors, batch migrations, coverage, greenfield scaffolds.
  • Provide backpressure the loop cannot skip: tests, typecheck, lint in hooks; otherwise it converges on nonsense.
  • Use a completion promise and a max-iteration/time bound; log every iteration's diff for morning review.
  • Do not run it on a shared mature codebase without a branch, a budget cap, and a reviewer step.
  • Prefer Claude Code's built-in /loop or /goal for in-session loops and routines for scheduled ones.
Where to learn (10 sources)
  • Ralph Wiggum plugin README Anthropic (claude-code repo), 2026, repo
  • The Ralph Wiggum Loop: Autonomous Code Generation with a Fresh Context codecentric, 2026, blog
  • Ralph Wiggum: Autonomous Loops for Claude Code paddo.dev, 2026, blog
  • 'Ralph Wiggum' loop prompts Claude to vibe-clone commercial software for $10 an hour The Register, 2026-01, blog
  • Keep Claude working toward a goal (/goal, /loop, Stop hook comparison) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Claude Code Routines: 5 Production Workflows + MCP Setup Arcade, 2026, blog

Names to know: Geoffrey Huntley, Ryan Carson, Matt Pocock

32Cross-tool protocols: MCP, A2A, AGENTS.md

establishedSeed topic 3
Search forModel Context ProtocolAgent2Agent protocolAgent CardA2A Task / Message / Artifact / PartAgentic AI Foundation (Linux Foundation)MCP for tools, A2A for agentsAGENTS.mdACP

What it is. MCP standardises agent-to-tool access (97M monthly SDK downloads by March 2026; every major vendor); A2A (Google, April 2025, now under the Linux Foundation's Agentic AI Foundation, v1.x, 150+ orgs in production) standardises agent-to-agent delegation with discoverable Agent Cards, Tasks with lifecycle states, Messages and Artifacts. AGENTS.md is the AAIF-stewarded instruction-file format read by 20+ tools; Claude Code reads CLAUDE.md and recommends importing AGENTS.md from it. For a solo developer, the protocol concepts (task with a lifecycle, artifact as the deliverable, card describing capabilities) transfer as vocabulary and structure even when nothing speaks A2A.

Why it matters for you. Mike does not need A2A today, but its Task/Artifact/Message split is a clean mental model for his own conventions, and MCP is how he would expose a non-Claude tool or a shared ledger to every session.

Best practices (3)
  • Structure your own handoffs like A2A: a task (id, state), messages (short, addressed), artifacts (files) - do not mix them.
  • If a shared board needs to be reachable from every session with one interface, expose it as an MCP server rather than a file everyone parses differently.
  • Keep CLAUDE.md as the Claude-specific layer and @AGENTS.md for anything other tools must also read.
Where to learn (9 sources)
  • Announcing the Agent2Agent Protocol (A2A) Google Developers, 2025-04, blog
  • Agent2Agent protocol (A2A) is getting an upgrade Google Cloud, 2025-07, blog

  • Google's A2A protocol gets a new home Axios, 2026-08, blog

  • MCP vs A2A: The Complete Guide to AI Agent Protocols in 2026 dev.to, 2026, blog

Names to know: Rao Surapaneni (Google, A2A), David Soria Parra and Justin Spahr-Summers (MCP creators)

33Framework handoff semantics: OpenAI Agents SDK, LangGraph, CrewAI, AutoGen

establishedSeed topic 3
Search forhandoff (OpenAI Agents SDK)transfer_to_agentinput_filterLangGraph Command(goto, update)supervisor / network / hierarchicalCrewAI delegationfull history vs final result onlyshared state schema

What it is. How the major frameworks move control and context between agents. OpenAI's SDK handoff passes the entire conversation to the next agent by default, with input_filter to trim it and a typed input_type for metadata; LangGraph models handoffs as Command objects moving between graph nodes with explicit, typed shared state, and offers supervisor, network and hierarchical topologies; CrewAI delegates between role-defined agents and inserts its own coordination prompts (chatty). The recurring design fork is: hand over full history (simple, tokens balloon) or hand over only the final result plus state (lean, must be explicit).

Why it matters for you. The full-history-vs-final-result choice is exactly copy-paste-the-spec vs send-a-summary-and-a-path; the frameworks have settled on typed state plus filtered history, which maps to spec file + short message.

Best practices (3)
  • Default to final-result-plus-state handoffs; only forward full history when the receiver truly needs the reasoning (Claude Code's fork is the equivalent).
  • Give the receiver a prompt prefix explaining the handoff protocol (OpenAI ships RECOMMENDED_PROMPT_PREFIX; the Claude Code harness injects a 'from another Claude' preface automatically).
  • Model shared state explicitly (a schema), not as an ever-growing message list.
Where to learn (5 sources)
  • OpenAI Agents SDK: Handoffs OpenAI, docs
  • OpenAI Agents SDK vs LangGraph (2026): Handoffs vs Durable Graphs Cipher Projects, 2026, blog
  • LangGraph vs CrewAI vs OpenAI Agents SDK: 2026 Guide Codebridge, 2026, blog
  • Choosing an agent framework: LangChain vs LangGraph vs CrewAI vs PydanticAI vs Mastra vs Vercel AI SDK Speakeasy, blog
  • Subagents in the SDK (Claude Agent SDK) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs

Names to know: Harrison Chase (LangChain), João Moura (CrewAI)

34Memory hierarchy as shared context: CLAUDE.md, rules, auto memory, subagent memory

establishedSeed topic 2
Search forCLAUDE.md scopesCLAUDE.local.md.claude/rules/ with paths frontmatter@importsauto memory MEMORY.mdmemory: project frontmatter for subagentswhat survives compaction/context

What it is. Claude Code's built-in layers of persistent context that every session (and every teammate) loads: managed -> user -> project -> local CLAUDE.md files (concatenated), path-scoped rules, @imported files, and auto memory (per repo, shared across worktrees, first 200 lines/25KB of MEMORY.md loaded). Subagents can be given their own memory scope. Project-root CLAUDE.md is re-injected after compaction; conversation-only instructions are not.

Why it matters for you. This is the cheapest cross-session channel Mike already has: conventions, the ownership map, the location of the spec directory and the status ledger belong in CLAUDE.md or a rule, so every specialist starts knowing where to look and where to write.

Best practices (4)
  • Put the handoff protocol itself in CLAUDE.md or .claude/rules/handoff.md: where specs live, the status schema, file ownership, when to message.
  • Keep CLAUDE.md under ~200 lines; move procedures to skills and area-specific rules to path-scoped rules.
  • Do not use CLAUDE.md for volatile status (it changes too often); use the ledger/handoff files for that.
  • Use /context to confirm what each session actually loaded; use the InstructionsLoaded hook to debug.
Where to learn (4 sources)
  • How Claude remembers your project Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Best practices for Claude Code (Write an effective CLAUDE.md) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Create custom subagents (memory field, what loads at startup) Claude Code Docs, 2026-09, docs
  • Context Engineering: CLAUDE.md and .cursorrules robbyb910 (Substack), blog

Names to know: Anthropic Claude Code team

35Scheduled cloud agents ('routines' / 'automations')

emergingSeed topic 4
Search forClaude Code routines/scheduleCursor AutomationsCodex scheduled tasksDevin Scheduled Sessionscloud agentscron-triggered LLM agentset-and-forget automation

What it is. Every major coding-agent vendor now ships a first-class way to run a saved prompt against a repo on a cron schedule (or API/webhook trigger) in their own cloud. Anthropic's Routines (research preview, April 14 2026) run full autonomous Claude Code sessions on Anthropic infrastructure: a prompt + repos + connectors, triggered by schedule (minimum 1 hour), API POST, or GitHub PR/release events, pushing to claude/-prefixed branches. Cursor Automations, OpenAI Codex scheduled tasks (standalone vs thread), and Devin Scheduled Sessions/Automations are the direct equivalents.

Why it matters for you. This is the productised version of the custodian idea: Mike can literally write 'every 2 hours, read commits since last run, update the glossary, post conflicts' as a routine prompt. The hourly floor on cloud routines and the fresh-clone (no local state) model shape the design.

Best practices (7)
  • Prompt must be self-contained and explicit about what success looks like; the routine runs with no approval prompts
  • Scope repos, environment network access, and connectors to what the routine actually needs (all connectors are included by default; remove the rest)
  • Claude pushes to claude/-prefixed branches; pushes to protected branches or branches with others' commits are rejected, so design for PR-based delivery
  • Green run status only means the session exited cleanly, not that the task succeeded; read the transcript
  • Cloud routines have a 1-hour minimum interval and daily run caps (Pro 5 / Max 15 / Team-Enterprise 25 per day); one-off runs do not count
  • Codex: test prompts manually first, prefer skills for maintainable workflows, start with minimal sandbox access
  • Cursor: be specific about actions, reference enabled tools, include decision rules and quality thresholds, specify output format
Where to learn (10 sources)
  • Automate work with routines (official docs) Anthropic, 2026-08, docs
  • Introducing routines in Claude Code Anthropic, 2026-04, blog
  • Anthropic Introduces Routines for Claude Code Automation Daniel Dominguez / InfoQ, 2026-05, blog
  • Claude Code Routines Tutorial: Schedule, API, and GitHub Triggers Explained Matt Abrams / Builder.io, 2026-04, blog
  • Claude Code Routines: The Complete Guide to Scheduled Cloud Agents (2026) Makerkit, 2026, blog
  • Automations | Cursor Docs Cursor, 2026, docs

Names to know: Anthropic Claude Code team, Matt Abrams (Builder.io), Cursor, OpenAI Codex, Cognition (Devin)

36Continuous AI and GitHub Agentic Workflows (gh-aw)

emergingSeed topic 4
Search forContinuous AIagentic workflowsgh-awclaude-code-actionscheduled GitHub Actions with an LLMsafe outputsagentic CI

What it is. GitHub Next coined 'Continuous AI' for AI woven into the SDLC the way CI/CD is, and shipped GitHub Agentic Workflows (technical preview Feb 13 2026): Markdown-with-frontmatter workflows compiled into GitHub Actions that run Copilot, Claude Code, Codex, Gemini or Pi agents on schedules or repo events. Anthropic's claude-code-action is the Claude-specific ancestor, with a documented 'Scheduled Repository Maintenance' recipe (weekly cron, creates a summary issue). Both are the 'run the custodian inside CI' option.

Why it matters for you. If Mike's repos are on GitHub, a cron workflow is the cheapest, most auditable place to run a custodian, and gh-aw's 'safe outputs' model is the best-articulated safety design for exactly this job.

Best practices (5)
  • Read-only GitHub permissions by default; writes go through 'safe outputs' (pre-approved operations such as create-issue, comment, open-PR) applied in a separate job with scoped permissions
  • Sandboxed execution, tool allowlisting, network isolation, and threat detection for prompt injection
  • claude-code-action example: cron '0 0 * * 0', --allowedTools 'Read,Bash(npm:*),Bash(gh issue:*),Bash(git:*)', output = one summary issue
  • Use workflow_dispatch alongside schedule so you can run it manually while iterating
  • Reports and summaries go to issues; code changes go to branches/PRs; never both silently
Where to learn (7 sources)
  • Automate repository tasks with GitHub Agentic Workflows Don Syme & Peli de Halleux / GitHub Next, 2026-02, blog
  • GitHub Agentic Workflows are now in technical preview GitHub, 2026-02, blog
  • github/gh-aw repository GitHub Next, 2026, repo
  • Agentic Workflows project page GitHub Next, 2026, docs
  • claude-code-action solutions guide (Scheduled Repository Maintenance) Anthropic, 2026, repo
  • anthropics/claude-code-action Anthropic, 2026, repo

Names to know: Don Syme, Peli de Halleux, GitHub Next, Microsoft Research

37Headless Claude Code (claude -p) and the Agent SDK on cron

establishedSeed topic 4
Search forheadless modeprint mode-p flag--output-format jsonClaude Agent SDKclaude-code-sdkunattended agentDIY background agent

What it is. The build-it-yourself option: run `claude -p '<prompt>'` from cron, a systemd timer, Windows Task Scheduler, or a small orchestrator, or drive the same CLI from the Python/TypeScript Agent SDK. This is how people ran custodians before Routines existed and remains the way to get sub-hourly cadence, local filesystem access, custom state files, and your own logging. Open-source control planes like ColeMurray/background-agents wrap this with sandboxes, cron/Sentry/webhook triggers, and multi-repo fan-out.

Why it matters for you. A 2-hour cadence with access to all local repos and a persistent 'last seen commit' file is easiest to build as a headless run on Mike's own machine; Routines are cloud-only fresh clones.

Best practices (7)
  • Always set --max-turns and --max-budget-usd for unattended runs; treat hitting max-turns as 'task mis-scoped', not 'retry'
  • Use --output-format json and parse it as JSON (jq); tee raw output to a log file first
  • Whitelist tools with --allowedTools (a read-only reviewer needs only Read,Grep,Glob); use --permission-mode dontAsk so unapproved tools auto-deny instead of hanging
  • Capture session_id from the JSON so a failed run can be --resume'd; use deterministic --session-id for idempotent retries
  • Wrap each run in a wall-clock timeout and a concurrency lock so two custodian runs never overlap
  • Route routine work to a cheaper model (--model sonnet) and reserve Opus for judgment-heavy jobs
  • Ask yourself: 'if this runs at 3am and does exactly what the prompt literally says, am I comfortable with no review?'
Where to learn (6 sources)
  • Claude Code in CI/CD and Headless Automation Hidekazu Konishi, 2026-08, blog
  • How to Use Claude Code Cron Jobs to Run Agents on a Schedule MindStudio, 2026, blog
  • Claude Code Headless Mode: The Complete Self-Hosting Guide (2026) amux, 2026, blog
  • ColeMurray/background-agents (open-source background agents system) Cole Murray, 2026, repo
  • How Do I Use Claude Code Safely in CI/CD Pipelines with Budget Controls? BSWEN, 2026-03, blog
  • AgentsRoom Scheduled Tasks: cron-style scheduling for AI coding agents AgentsRoom, 2026, docs

Names to know: Hidekazu Konishi, Cole Murray

38Local scheduling: Desktop scheduled tasks, /loop, loop.md

emergingSeed topic 4
Search forClaude Code Desktop scheduled tasks/looploop.mdCronCreatein-session cronbuilt-in maintenance promptScheduleWakeup

What it is. Anthropic offers three schedulers: cloud Routines (1h minimum, fresh clone), Desktop scheduled tasks (run on your machine with local files, 1-minute minimum, optional isolated worktree per run, per-task permission mode, catch-up runs after sleep), and the session-scoped /loop skill (CronCreate/CronList/CronDelete tools, 7-day expiry, jitter). A bare /loop runs a built-in maintenance prompt: continue unfinished work, tend the branch's PR, then cleanup passes; .claude/loop.md replaces it with your own custodian prompt.

Why it matters for you. Desktop scheduled tasks are the closest match to 'every 2 hours, read all my local repos' without building anything; loop.md is a ready-made place to encode a per-project custodian routine.

Best practices (6)
  • Desktop tasks run against the working directory as-is, including uncommitted changes; enable the worktree toggle to isolate each run
  • Missed runs collapse into one catch-up run, so write prompts that are time-aware ('only review today's commits; if after 5pm just summarise')
  • Click Run now after creating a task and 'always allow' the tools it needs, or Manual-mode runs will stall on permission prompts
  • Avoid :00/:30 minute slots if exact timing matters (deterministic jitter up to 30 min)
  • Session /loop tasks expire after 7 days and only fire while the session is idle; use them for babysitting, not for durable custodial work
  • A task can reschedule itself via update_scheduled_task based on what it finds
Where to learn (4 sources)
  • Schedule recurring tasks in Claude Code Desktop Anthropic, 2026, docs
  • Run prompts on a schedule (/loop) Anthropic, 2026, docs
  • Claude Code Loops & Agents: /goal, /loop, /schedule Tutorial Robert Mill, 2026-07, blog
  • Claude Code Scheduled Tasks: Complete Setup Guide (2026) claudefa.st, 2026, blog

Names to know: Robert Mill

39Janitor / tech-debt-burner agents

establishedSeed topic 4
Search forjanitor agentnightly tech-debt burnerrepo hygiene botcodebase gardeningsoftware gardeningdead code removal agentless code = less debt

What it is. The 'custodian' archetype as practitioners describe it: an agent whose whole job is deletion and simplification. GitHub's awesome-copilot ships a 'Universal Janitor' agent definition (dead code, unused imports, duplicate logic, obsolete tests, outdated comments, IaC cruft) with rules like 'measure before acting' and 'simplify one concept at a time'. Kinde's 'Nightly Tech-Debt Burners' lays out the architecture (scheduler, agentic core, guardrails, PR) and a four-week phased rollout. The pre-LLM lineage is 'software gardening' and Dependabot/Renovate.

Why it matters for you. Gives Mike a ready catalogue of low-risk custodial jobs and, more importantly, the rollout discipline (observe first, then limited PRs, then auto-merge only for proven job types).

Best practices (5)
  • Phase it: week 1 read-only observation/report, week 2 small PRs with max-diff guardrails, week 3 expanded scope with test thresholds, week 4 auto-merge only for proven low-risk classes
  • Cap diff size per PR; one concept per change; validate continuously (tests, lint, build) after each removal
  • Agents excel at objective, systematic work (dead code, renames, syntax modernisation), not creative refactors
  • A comprehensive test suite is the real safety net; without it, keep the janitor read-only
  • Never grant unnecessary permissions; start restrictive and widen per job type
Where to learn (6 sources)
  • Nightly Tech-Debt Burners: Scheduling Agents to Clean Your Repo Kinde, blog
  • awesome-copilot janitor.agent.md GitHub, 2026, repo
  • Meet Janitor: The Essential AI Agent Maintenance Tool for OpenClaw aloycwl / DEV, 2026, blog
  • Agentic AI with LangChain for Dead Code Removal Changepond, 2025, blog
  • Software Gardening (GitHub org) Software Gardening, repo
  • The autonomous codebase LeadDev, 2025, blog

40Memory consolidation ('auto-dream') and CLAUDE.md upkeep

emergingSeed topic 4
Search forauto-dream/dreammemory consolidationdream-skillMEMORY.md pruningCLAUDE.md driftAGENTS.md configuration smellsobsidian-second-brain scheduled agents

What it is. Anthropic built a background 'dream' agent into Claude Code that, after 24h and 5+ sessions, runs a four-phase pass over the memory directory: orient, gather signal (grep transcripts for corrections and decisions), consolidate (merge, absolutise dates, delete contradicted facts), then prune and re-index MEMORY.md under ~200 lines. It is read-only on project code and uses a lock file to prevent concurrent runs. Community replicas (grandamenium/dream-skill) and vault-maintenance systems (obsidian-second-brain's morning/nightly/weekly/health agents) apply the same idea to knowledge bases. A 2025 paper catalogues 'configuration smells' in AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md files, motivating periodic audits.

Why it matters for you. Mike's 'update the glossary when new concepts appear' is this pattern applied to a glossary: gather signal from recent commits, merge into topic files, keep the index lean. Anthropic's own prompt is the best template.

Best practices (7)
  • Gather signal selectively (grep for decisions/corrections/new terms) rather than re-reading everything
  • Merge over duplicate: improve existing entries instead of creating near-duplicates
  • Convert relative dates to absolute; when two files disagree, fix the wrong source
  • Keep the index one line per entry (~150 chars) and under ~200 lines; demote verbose content into topic files
  • Read-only on code; only the memory/glossary directory is writable; take a lock so runs never overlap
  • Ship maintenance agents opt-in and restricted (obsidian-second-brain's background agent can add/update but not delete or merge)
  • Periodically audit CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md for stale instructions, contradictions, and content that should be a skill
Where to learn (7 sources)
  • Claude Code Dreams: Anthropic's New Memory Feature claudefa.st, 2026, blog
  • Dream memory consolidation system prompt (extracted) Piebald-AI, 2026, repo
  • grandamenium/dream-skill grandamenium, 2026, repo
  • Does Claude Code Need Sleep? Inside the Unreleased Auto-dream Feature akari_iku / DEV, 2026, blog
  • obsidian-second-brain (scheduled agents that maintain the vault) Eugeniu Ghelbur, 2026, repo
  • Configuration Smells in AGENTS.md Files dos Santos, Costa, Montandon, Silva, Valente, 2026, paper

Names to know: Anthropic Claude Code team, Piebald-AI, Marco Tulio Valente

41Parallel-workstream conflict detection

emergingSeed topic 4
Search forClashgit merge-treeworktree conflict predictionsemantic conflictshotspot filesparallel agents same repomerge conflict early warning

What it is. When several agent sessions work the same repo, textual merge conflicts are the easy case; duplicated implementations and 'semantic contradictions' (compiles, passes tests, disagrees at runtime) are the hard ones. Clash (open source, Rust) runs read-only three-way merges via git merge-tree between every worktree pair and reports a conflict matrix, with a Claude Code hook mode, watch TUI, and JSON output. Augment Code's guide names four overlap failure modes and the coordinator/specialist/verifier split; a contrarian essay argues coordination cost grows quadratically with agent count.

Why it matters for you. This is the 'detect conflicts between features developed in parallel' half of Mike's idea, and Clash's JSON output is something a custodian can call every 2 hours and post to the bulletin board.

Best practices (6)
  • Give each session its own worktree/branch so conflicts surface at merge points, not mid-edit
  • Run a conflict scan (git merge-tree / clash status --json) on a schedule and post the matrix; do not try to auto-resolve
  • Watch hotspot files (routes, configs, registries, schema files) explicitly; most collisions concentrate there
  • Look beyond text: flag two branches touching the same function/API surface or adding similar helpers (duplicate implementations)
  • Merge sequentially with rebases rather than in a batch
  • Spec-driven decomposition with explicit file/interface boundaries prevents more conflicts than any detector
Where to learn (6 sources)
  • clash-sh/clash: Avoid merge conflicts across git worktrees for parallel AI coding agents clash-sh, 2026, repo
  • Show HN: I built Clash to avoid conflicts when running AI agents in parallel Hacker News, 2026, thread
  • How to Run a Multi-Agent Coding Workspace (2026) Augment Code, 2026-06, blog
  • Stop parallelizing your AI agents Dave Paola, 2026-02, blog
  • Git Worktrees + Claude Code: The 2026 Playbook for Running Parallel Agents Developers Digest, 2026, blog
  • Running Coding Agents in Parallel with Git Worktrees Andrea Schiona / DEV, 2026, blog

Names to know: clash-sh author, Dave Paola, Augment Code

42Blackboard / bulletin-board coordination

emergingSeed topic 4
Search forblackboard architecturebMASshared task documentbulletin boardMetaGPT message poolstigmergyshared markdown state fileWork Context Protocol

What it is. The classic AI 'blackboard' pattern (agents coordinate indirectly through a shared, structured workspace rather than direct messages) has been revived for LLM multi-agent systems (Google's blackboard MAS for data science, bMAS, MetaGPT's message pool). In coding practice it shows up as a shared task document or markdown board that every session reads, claims tasks on, and posts findings to. Mike's 'bulletin board of noticed issues' is precisely a blackboard written by the custodian and read by the working sessions.

Why it matters for you. Names the thing and gives him the design rules: append-only entries, structured fields, and 'read before you start' as a CLAUDE.md instruction for every session.

Best practices (5)
  • Keep the board in-repo as a markdown/JSON file so every session sees it at startup (reference it from CLAUDE.md)
  • Structured entries: timestamp, source run, severity, affected files/branches, suggested owner, status; make them greppable
  • Custodian appends and resolves; humans/sessions acknowledge; nobody silently deletes
  • Expire or archive stale entries so the board does not become noise
  • Separate 'observations' (conflicts, drift) from 'tasks' (claimable work) to avoid agents grabbing observations as todos
Where to learn (5 sources)
  • LLM-Based Multi-Agent Blackboard System for Information Discovery in Data Science Google Research, 2025-10, paper
  • Exploring Advanced LLM Multi-Agent Systems Based on Blackboard Architecture arXiv, 2025-07, paper
  • Beyond Self-Talk: A Communication-Centric Survey of LLM-Based Multi-Agent Systems arXiv, 2025-02, paper
  • How to Build a Multi-Agent AI System for Code Development Augment Code, 2026, blog
  • Multi-agent Collaboration with State Management arXiv, 2026-05, paper

Names to know: Google Research blackboard MAS authors, MetaGPT

43Docs drift detection and living documentation

establishedSeed topic 4
Search fordocs driftdocumentation syncself-updating docsDeepWikiDevin WikiMintlify WorkflowsSwimm auto-syncdocs-as-code agent

What it is. Detecting that documentation no longer matches code and fixing it is the canonical scheduled-agent job: Anthropic lists 'docs drift' as a routine example (weekly, scan merged PRs since last run, open update PRs), claude-code-action has a docs-sync recipe, Mintlify's agent clones repos and opens docs PRs on merge/release/weekly triggers, Swimm flags stale docs in CI via code-coupled tokens, and Cognition's DeepWiki re-indexes repos every couple of hours to regenerate architecture wikis.

Why it matters for you. A project glossary is a documentation artifact; the same 'diff since last run, find affected docs, propose an edit' loop applies directly, and DeepWiki is proof that re-indexing every few hours is normal.

Best practices (5)
  • Diff-driven: only look at what changed since the last run, then find docs that reference changed symbols/APIs
  • Open PRs for humans to review rather than pushing to docs directly (Mintlify default; direct push is opt-in)
  • Couple docs to code where possible (Swimm-style tokens, doctest-style executable examples) so drift is detectable deterministically
  • Regenerate overviews/wikis wholesale (DeepWiki) but edit hand-written docs surgically
  • Weekly cadence is the common default for docs drift; hourly is overkill
Where to learn (7 sources)
  • Docs on autopilot: From zero to self-maintaining with Mintlify Mintlify, 2026-04, blog
  • How to Stop Documentation Drift Mintlify, 2026, blog
  • Self-Updating Documentation: Docs Agents Keep in Sync Augment Code, 2026, blog
  • DeepWiki - Devin Docs Cognition, 2026, docs
  • DeepWiki: AI docs for any repo Cognition, 2025, blog
  • Inside DeepWiki: How Cognition Builds Wikis for Devin at Scale YouTube (channel not captured), video

Names to know: Mintlify, Swimm, Cognition / DeepWiki

44ADR and changelog generation from commits

emerging
Search forarchitecture decision recordsgit-adradr-agentautomated ADRchangelog generationconventional commitschangelogenrelease notes agent

What it is. Two well-trodden custodial writing jobs. Changelogs: tools from changelogen/git-cliff (deterministic, from conventional commits) to LLM generators (ChangelogAI, trigger.dev auto-changelog) turn commit ranges into release notes. ADRs: git-adr drafts an ADR from each commit diff with Claude; an EASE 2026 paper shows LLM ADR generation improves markedly when given the last 3-5 prior ADRs as context, and earlier work found GPT-4 zero-shot decisions relevant but below human level.

Why it matters for you. Both are natural 'read commits since last run' outputs, and the 3-5-prior-records finding is a concrete context-window recipe for any glossary/ADR-drafting custodian.

Best practices (5)
  • Draft, do not decide: ADRs land as 'proposed' PRs or bulletin-board entries for a human to accept
  • Feed the last 3-5 existing records as context; full history adds cost without quality
  • Prefer deterministic generation (conventional commits) for the changelog skeleton and use the LLM only to summarise/group
  • Trigger ADR drafting on signals (new dependency, schema change, new top-level module), not on every commit
  • Keep a stable template so generated records are diffable across runs
Where to learn (6 sources)
  • Context Matters: Evaluating Context Strategies for Automated ADR Generation Using LLMs Gupta, Dhar, Feitosa, Vaidhyanathan (EASE 2026), 2026-04, paper
  • kaldiflow/git-adr: AI-generated ADRs from git diffs kaldiflow, 2026, repo
  • From Stale Docs to Living Architecture: Automating ADRs with GitHub + LLM Iraj Hedayati, blog
  • Can LLMs Generate Architectural Design Decisions? An Exploratory Empirical Study arXiv, 2024-03, paper

  • Generate a changelog from GitHub commits using AI Trigger.dev, repo
