Dex Horthy / HumanLayer CEO of HumanLayer; author of 12-Factor Agents and 'Advanced Context Engineering for Coding Agents'; coined the 'dumb zone' and the Research -> Plan -> Implement workflow; often credited with 'context engineering', a claim contested with Karpathy, Tobi Lutke and LangChain.
See also: 12-factor agents, Dumb zone, Research -> Plan -> Implement (RPI), Context engineering		 People & orgs (eponyms) 2026-09-03 12-factor-agents · Advanced Context Engineering for Coding Agents (Dex Horthy talk notes, 'dumb zone') · Context engineering with Dex Horthy
Docs drift Documentation no longer matching code; detecting and fixing it is the canonical scheduled-agent job (Anthropic's routine example: weekly, scan merged PRs, open update PRs; Mintlify and Swimm do the same). Cognition's DeepWiki shows wholesale regeneration every few hours is normal for overview docs.
See also: Custodian agent, Living documentation (ADRs and docs as agent memory), Routines		 Custodian & automation 2026-09-03 Automate work with routines · Docs on autopilot: From zero to self-maintaining with Mintlify · DeepWiki: AI docs for any repo
Dumb zone Dex Horthy's (HumanLayer) label for the region of the context window, starting around 40% full, where output quality drops noticeably before the hard limit. Geoffrey Huntley observed a similar clip around 147-152k of a 200k window; the fix is to compact or split at 40-60%, not 95%.
See also: Context rot, Compaction, Session splitting (parallel sessions)		 Context management 2026-09-03 Advanced Context Engineering for Coding Agents (Dex Horthy talk notes, 'dumb zone') · Ralph Wiggum as a 'software engineer'
Duplicated work Two workers doing the same thing because decomposition was vague or nobody claimed the task; Anthropic's Research team hit it with overlapping searches, and claim-before-work plus clear briefs are the fix.
See also: Claim-before-work, Task brief, Decompose by context boundary		 Failure modes 2026-09-03 How we built our multi-agent research system · When to use multi-agent systems (and when not to)
Dynamic workflows (ultracode) May 2026 research preview: Claude writes a JavaScript script calling agent(), pipeline() and parallel() to run tens to hundreds of subagents; the script, not Claude's context, holds intermediate results and can make agents cross-check each other. Triggered with 'ultracode:' or /effort ultracode; limits 16 concurrent, 1,000 agents per run; Jarred Sumner used it to port Bun from Zig to Rust.
See also: Fan-out / fan-in, Subagents, Schema-validated handoff		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 Orchestrate subagents at scale with dynamic workflows · Introducing dynamic workflows in Claude Code (Bun port)
Effort levels /effort low/medium/high/xhigh/max (plus ultracode) trades thinking tokens for quality; extended thinking is on by default in Claude Code and 'ultrathink' was the older prompt-word for maximum reasoning. Raise it for hard debugging and async runs, lower it for mechanical tasks; changing it mid-session invalidates the cache.
See also: Model routing by role, Dynamic workflows (ultracode)		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 Manage costs effectively · Pricing (Claude Platform docs)
Erik Schluntz and Barry Zhang (Anthropic) Co-authors of Anthropic's 'Building Effective Agents' (December 2024), the most-cited practical agent taxonomy (workflows vs agents, the five patterns, augmented LLM); Zhang also co-authored the multi-agent research system post and co-created Agent Skills.
See also: Building Effective Agents patterns, Orchestrator-workers		 People & orgs (eponyms) 2026-09-03 Building Effective AI Agents · How we built our multi-agent research system
Error analysis Hamel Husain's first step before building any grader: read 100+ traces, write notes, cluster failure modes into a taxonomy, count. Prefer binary pass/fail judgments over 1-5 scales and appoint one domain expert as the quality 'benevolent dictator'.
See also: Evals, LLM-as-judge, Traces and spans		 Evals & observability 2026-09-03 AI Evals: Everything You Need to Know (FAQ)
Evals Systematic tests of whether your agent works on your tasks with your data, distinct from public benchmarks. Anthropic's 'Demystifying evals' distinguishes single-turn from multi-turn agent evals and three grader types (code-based, model-based, human), and recommends starting with 20-50 tasks drawn from real failures. Hamel Husain's course is aimed at engineers and PMs.
See also: Error analysis, LLM-as-judge, Golden runs		 Evals & observability 2026-09-03 Demystifying evals for AI agents · AI Evals: Everything You Need to Know (FAQ) · AI Evals For Engineers & PMs (Maven course)
Evaluator-optimizer Building Effective Agents workflow in which one LLM produces output and another evaluates it against criteria in a loop. It is the general form of the Writer/Reviewer pattern and of /goal's separate judge; Osmani generalizes it as 'separate generation from evaluation because models grade their own work positively'.
See also: Writer / Reviewer pattern, /goal, Adversarial review, Building Effective Agents patterns		 Multi-agent patterns 2026-09-03 Building Effective AI Agents · Long-running agents
Exchange Mike's term for one message from him plus one response from Claude; a single exchange may contain many turns on Claude's side. Deliberately not an industry term of art, chosen precisely because it is unambiguous where 'turn' is overloaded.
See also: Message, Turn, Session		 Working vocabulary 2026-09-03 Mike's global CLAUDE.md (Glossary section)
Extension decision matrix Anthropic's framework for which layer an instruction belongs in: CLAUDE.md for always-on facts, rules for path-scoped guidance, skills for on-demand procedures, hooks for things that must happen every time, subagents for context isolation, MCP for external systems, plugins for packaging. Triggers: wrong twice -> CLAUDE.md; same prompt repeatedly -> skill; must always happen -> hook.
See also: CLAUDE.md, Skills (SKILL.md), Hooks, Subagents, Write rules, don't repeat corrections		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 Extend Claude Code (features overview) · Steering Claude Code: when to use CLAUDE.md, skills, hooks, and subagents
Fan-out / fan-in Splitting one job into many parallel agent runs (fan-out) and merging their results (fan-in), map-reduce style. Claude Code offers claude -p loops, /batch (5-30 worktree-isolated subagents each opening a PR) and dynamic workflows for tens to hundreds of agents.
See also: Dynamic workflows (ultracode), Headless mode (claude -p), Git worktrees, Orchestrator-workers		 Multi-agent patterns 2026-09-03 Best practices for Claude Code · Orchestrate subagents at scale with dynamic workflows
File ownership partitioning Assigning each parallel agent exclusive files or directories so edits never collide; mandatory in agent teams (which lack worktrees) and good practice everywhere. When ownership cannot be guaranteed, use worktrees or advisory file leases.
See also: Single-writer principle, Git worktrees, Conflict matrix		 Multi-agent patterns 2026-09-03 Orchestrate teams of Claude Code sessions · The Code Agent Orchestra: what makes multi-agent coding work
File system as context Manus's (Yichao 'Peak' Ji) principle of treating the filesystem as the agent's unlimited, persistent memory: write large observations to files and keep only paths and summaries in the window. The 'context window is RAM, filesystem is disk' analogy comes from the same lineage.
See also: Structured note-taking, Repository as system of record, Artifact-plus-pointer handoff		 Context management 2026-09-03 Context Engineering for AI Agents: Lessons from Building Manus · The HANDOFF.md Pattern - How to Keep Claude Code Productive Across Sessions
Fork A branch off an existing session that inherits its context. In Claude Code this is /branch (copies the transcript) or --fork-session on the CLI; for subagents, /subtask forks a worker that inherits the whole conversation instead of starting fresh.
See also: Session, Subagents		 Working vocabulary 2026-09-03 Mike's global CLAUDE.md (Glossary section) · Manage sessions (name, resume, branch, fork) · CLI reference (--max-turns, --resume, --continue, --fork-session) · Create custom subagents
Fresh-context loop Any design where each iteration or phase starts with an empty context window and reconstructs state from files and git, so every iteration is a handoff between two agents that share only the filesystem. Ralph loops, RPI phases, Superpowers' per-task subagents and Anthropic's harness all use it.
See also: Ralph Wiggum loop, Intentional compaction, Progress file, Subagent-driven development		 Autonomy 2026-09-03 Ralph Wiggum as a 'software engineer' · Effective harnesses for long-running agents · Advanced Context Engineering for Coding Agents (Dex Horthy talk notes, 'dumb zone')
Full-history vs final-result handoff The recurring design fork in agent frameworks: hand the next agent the entire conversation (OpenAI Agents SDK default, Claude Code forks) or only the final result plus explicit state (LangGraph Command objects, Claude Code subagents). Full history is simple but tokens balloon; final-result is lean but state must be modeled explicitly.
See also: Handoff, Fork, Subagents, OpenAI Agents SDK		 Inter-agent communication 2026-09-03 OpenAI Agents SDK: Handoffs · Create custom subagents
Gas Town Steve Yegge's January 2026 multi-agent workspace manager layered on Beads, with named roles: Mayor coordinates, Polecats work in isolated worktrees, Refinery serializes merges, Witness monitors, Deacon handles housekeeping; work moves in 'convoys'. Influential, expensive (reported $100/hour burns) and, by Yegge's own account, chaotic.
See also: Beads, Team of coding agents, Steve Yegge		 Inter-agent communication 2026-09-03 Welcome to Gas Town · Gas Town (yegge.ai) · GasTown and the Two Kinds of Multi-Agent
Geoffrey Huntley Originator of the Ralph Wiggum loop (July 2025) and the term backpressure for the checks a loop cannot skip; observed Claude's quality clipping around 147-152k tokens of a 200k window.
See also: Ralph Wiggum loop, Backpressure, Dumb zone		 People & orgs (eponyms) 2026-09-03 Ralph Wiggum as a 'software engineer'
Git discipline Version control as the agent safety net: commit at every green checkpoint with messages that explain why, one branch per session or worktree, and let Claude read git log to answer 'why' questions. Every serious practitioner (Willison, Osmani, Anthropic's harness posts) lists it as a precondition.
See also: Repository as system of record, Git worktrees		 Engineering practice 2026-09-03 Vibe engineering · Effective harnesses for long-running agents · Checkpointing (rewind and summarize)
Git worktrees One checkout per session so parallel edits never collide: claude --worktree <name> creates .claude/worktrees/<name> on branch worktree-<name>, the desktop app does it automatically, subagents can set isolation: worktree, and .worktreeinclude copies .env files in. The documented pairing for hand-run parallel sessions is worktrees plus cross-session messaging.
See also: Session splitting (parallel sessions), File ownership partitioning, Conflict matrix		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 Run parallel sessions with worktrees · Best practices for Claude Code
GitHub Agentic Workflows (gh-aw) Technical preview (Feb 2026) of Markdown-with-frontmatter workflows compiled into GitHub Actions that run Copilot, Claude Code, Codex, Gemini or Pi agents on schedules or events, with read-only permissions by default, sandboxing, and writes gated through safe outputs.
See also: Continuous AI, Safe outputs		 Custodian & automation 2026-09-03 Automate repository tasks with GitHub Agentic Workflows · github/gh-aw README (safe outputs)
Golden rule of prompting Anthropic's first principle: treat Claude as a brilliant new employee who lacks your context, be explicit about the output, and explain the motivation behind constraints; if a colleague with minimal context would be confused by the prompt, Claude will be too.
See also: Prompt engineering, Positive instructions		 Prompting 2026-09-03 Prompting best practices · Best practices for Claude Code
Golden runs Fixture-based regression for a custodian or prompt: replay 5-10 historical commit ranges with known expected findings and diff the agent's output against the accepted answer whenever the prompt or model changes, scoring precision first because false conflicts are the expensive failure.
See also: Evals, Custodian agent		 Evals & observability 2026-09-03 SWE-CI: Evaluating Agent Capabilities in Maintaining Codebases via Continuous Integration
Guides vs sensors Bockeler's control-systems framing of a harness: guides (feedforward) prevent bad behavior, sensors (feedback) detect it, and each is either computational (linters, tests, types, fitness functions) or inferential (model review). Prefer computational where possible; invest first in the maintainability harness.
See also: Harness engineering, Verification loop, Hooks		 Engineering practice 2026-09-03 Harness engineering for coding agent users
Hamel Husain Independent consultant (Parlance Labs) and, with Shreya Shankar, teacher of 'AI Evals for Engineers & PMs'; the reference voice for error analysis first, binary pass/fail and validating LLM judges against humans.
See also: Evals, Error analysis, LLM-as-judge		 People & orgs (eponyms) 2026-09-03 AI Evals: Everything You Need to Know (FAQ) · AI Evals For Engineers & PMs (Maven course)
Handoff Transferring work and its necessary context from one agent, session or iteration to another. Every handoff is lossy unless the receiver can read the same files; the frameworks' design fork is full history versus final result plus state.
See also: Artifact-plus-pointer handoff, Telephone game, HANDOFF.md pattern, Full-history vs final-result handoff		 Working vocabulary 2026-09-03 OpenAI Agents SDK: Handoffs · The HANDOFF.md Pattern - How to Keep Claude Code Productive Across Sessions · When to use multi-agent systems (and when not to)
HANDOFF.md pattern A per-session, timestamped markdown file with fixed sections (Goal, Done with paths, Did NOT work, Current state, Key decisions, Next step) written at session end or before compaction and read at session start, plus an append-only INDEX.md. It replaces the single shared file, which is last-writer-wins.
See also: Progress file, Last-writer-wins, Hooks		 Inter-agent communication 2026-09-03 The HANDOFF.md Pattern - How to Keep Claude Code Productive Across Sessions · planning-with-files (repo)
Harness Everything around the model that makes it behave reliably: tools, instruction files, verification loops, memory, guardrails, observability. Mitchell Hashimoto's 2026 slogan is 'Agent = Model + Harness'; Anthropic says Claude Code's harness is built from CLAUDE.md, hooks, skills, plugins and MCP.
See also: Harness engineering, Agent, CLAUDE.md, Hooks		 Working vocabulary 2026-09-03 Harness Engineering: Making AI Coding Agents Work in 2026 (Agent = Model + Harness) · Harness engineering for coding agent users · Set up Claude Code in a monorepo or large codebase
Harness engineering The 2026 discipline of building the environment around a model so it behaves reliably: instruction files, tools, verification loops, memory, guardrails, observability. Named in OpenAI's Codex team post (3 engineers, ~1M lines, zero hand-written code) and Birgitta Bockeler's martinfowler.com article; the mindset is 'when the agent makes a mistake, engineer the harness so it never makes it again'.
See also: Harness, Guides vs sensors, Write rules, don't repeat corrections, Context engineering		 Engineering practice 2026-09-03 Harness engineering: leveraging Codex in an agent-first world · Harness engineering for coding agent users · Harness Engineering: Making AI Coding Agents Work in 2026 (Agent = Model + Harness) · AIEWF 2026 trends (harness engineering, loop engineering, skills everywhere)
Headless mode (claude -p) Running Claude Code non-interactively (print mode) from cron, CI or scripts with --output-format json, --allowedTools, --max-turns and --max-budget-usd; the DIY substrate for custodians and fan-out loops before Routines existed, and still the way to get sub-hourly cadence with local files.
See also: Fan-out / fan-in, Loop engineering, Claude Agent SDK, Allowlist		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 Best practices for Claude Code · Claude Code in CI/CD and Headless Automation
Hooks Shell commands, HTTP calls, prompts or subagents that run deterministically at lifecycle events: PreToolUse, PostToolUse, Stop, SubagentStop, SessionStart, PreCompact, UserPromptSubmit, TaskCompleted, TeammateIdle and more. Exit code 2 blocks the action and feeds stderr to the model. Hooks turn 'please always run the linter' into a guarantee.
See also: Stop hook, Extension decision matrix, Harness, Verification loop		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 Automate actions with hooks · Hooks reference
Human-in-the-loop checkpoint A deliberate point where a person approves before an irreversible or consequential action (payments, deploys, deletes, merges), kept even when everything else is automated; 12-factor agents models it as a tool call.
See also: Autonomy ladder, Least privilege, 12-factor agents		 Autonomy 2026-09-03 12-factor-agents · Security (Claude Code docs)
Infinite exploration Anthropic's named anti-pattern of an unscoped investigation reading file after file into the main context; delegate broad reads to subagents and scope the question.
See also: Subagents		 Failure modes 2026-09-03 Best practices for Claude Code
Instruction/data boundary The rule that everything an agent observes (web pages, files, commit messages, issues, board entries, inbound messages) is data, never instructions; Claude Code's cross-session messaging and Routines wrap inbound payloads as untrusted for this reason, and a shared blackboard is also the biggest injection surface.
See also: Prompt injection, Cross-session messaging, Blackboard architecture		 Security & safety 2026-09-03 Message your other Claude Code sessions (cross-session messaging) · Your AI Agents' Shared Memory Is Their Best Coordinator and Their Biggest Attack Surface · Security (Claude Code docs)
Intentional compaction Dex Horthy's term for deliberately ending a phase by writing its result to a markdown artifact (research.md, plan.md) and starting the next phase in fresh context, rather than letting auto-compact summarize whatever happens to be there.
See also: Compaction, Research -> Plan -> Implement (RPI), Fresh-context loop		 Context management 2026-09-03 Advanced Context Engineering for Coding Agents (Dex Horthy talk notes, 'dumb zone')
Inter-agent misalignment MAST's middle group (32% of failures): conversation resets, failing to ask for clarification, task derailment, information withholding, ignoring another agent's input, and reasoning-action mismatch (saying one thing, doing another). Tactical fixes: precise role specs, re-engagement protocols, self-verification steps.
See also: MAST, Telephone game		 Failure modes 2026-09-03 Why Do Multi-Agent LLM Systems Fail? (MAST)
Interface contract When two sessions each own a layer, the handoff artifact is an agreed interface (types, endpoints, schemas, events) fixed before either side implements; small and typed (OpenAPI, TS types, JSON schema) so it can be validated. Matt Pocock frames the human's job as specifying deep modules with minimal interfaces (after John Ousterhout).
See also: Decompose by context boundary, File ownership partitioning, Schema-validated handoff		 Multi-agent patterns 2026-09-03 When to use multi-agent systems (and when not to) · Orchestrate teams of Claude Code sessions · Full Walkthrough: Workflow for AI Coding - Matt Pocock
Interview-me (question-first method) Give a one-line brief and have Claude interview you with its AskUserQuestion tool until a spec is complete, then write SPEC.md and implement in a fresh session. Anthropic ships the prompt in its docs; Radek Sienkiewicz's January 2026 post (32 questions, 157-line spec) popularized it after Anthropic's Thariq shared it. Matt Pocock's /grill-me is the argumentative variant.
See also: Spec, Plan mode, Brain dump to brief, Meta-prompting		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 Best practices for Claude Code · Stop prompting Claude Code - let it interview you · mattpocock/skills
Issue triage agent The most mature scheduled-agent job: classify, dedupe, label and first-respond to issues (Dosu, Cognition's !triage-bug playbook, Anthropic's backlog-grooming routine). Dosu's better-stale-bot replaces timestamp-only stale bots with one that reads the thread, closes only on evidence, and has grace periods and output caps.
See also: Custodian agent, Bot fatigue, Safe outputs		 Custodian & automation 2026-09-03 Introducing better-stale-bot, an AI GitHub Stale Bot That Reads First
Janitor agent The custodian archetype whose whole job is deletion and simplification: GitHub's awesome-copilot ships a 'Universal Janitor' definition (dead code, unused imports, duplicate logic, stale comments) with rules like 'measure before acting'; Kinde's 'Nightly Tech-Debt Burners' lays out the scheduler -> agent -> guardrails -> PR architecture and a four-week rollout from read-only to auto-merge.
See also: Custodian agent, PR-not-commit, Safe outputs		 Custodian & automation 2026-09-03 awesome-copilot janitor.agent.md · Nightly Tech-Debt Burners: Scheduling Agents to Clean Your Repo
Just-in-time context Giving the agent lightweight pointers (file paths, identifiers) and letting it read what it needs at run time, instead of pre-loading everything into CLAUDE.md or the prompt. Anthropic prefers it over pre-loading; Boris Cherny calls the same stance context minimalism.
See also: Progressive disclosure, Context minimalism		 Context management 2026-09-03 Effective context engineering for AI agents · How Boris Cherny uses Claude Code (132+ tips)
Kitchen-sink session Anthropic's named anti-pattern of one session accumulating unrelated tasks until irrelevant history crowds out the current instruction; /clear between tasks and one session per workstream are the fixes.

LangGraph LangChain's graph-based agent runtime (nodes, edges, typed shared state, checkpoints, human-in-the-loop) that models handoffs as Command objects and offers supervisor, network and hierarchical topologies; the framework enterprises most often standardize on, and the target of Anthropic's 'avoid frameworks' stance.
See also: Orchestrator-workers, Full-history vs final-result handoff		 Protocols & frameworks 2026-09-03 langchain-ai/langgraph
Last-writer-wins The failure of a single shared status file that every session edits: the most recent save silently overwrites others and nobody knows what is current. The community's fix is per-session files plus an append-only index, or a real ledger.
See also: Stale shared state, HANDOFF.md pattern		 Inter-agent communication 2026-09-03 The HANDOFF.md Pattern - How to Keep Claude Code Productive Across Sessions
Least privilege Give an agent only the tools, files, credentials and network it needs for the job: read-only reporters, no delete-capable tokens, no production access for unattended runs, per-job allowlists and hooks as hard vetoes. Read-only by default is the same principle stated as a starting posture.
See also: Allowlist, Sandboxing		 Security & safety 2026-09-03 Security (Claude Code docs) · Claude Code in CI/CD and Headless Automation · Nightly Tech-Debt Burners: Scheduling Agents to Clean Your Repo
Lethal trifecta Simon Willison's name for the combination that makes an agent exploitable: access to private data, exposure to untrusted content, and a way to communicate externally. Remove any one leg and prompt injection cannot exfiltrate.
See also: Prompt injection, Least privilege, Simon Willison		 Security & safety 2026-09-03 Simon Willison: about (coined 'prompt injection' and 'lethal trifecta')
Living documentation (ADRs and docs as agent memory) Treating the docs directory as the agent's map of the codebase (OpenAI's harness write-up) and Architecture Decision Records (Nygard's Status/Context/Decision/Consequences format) as the durable 'why' an agent reads before making architectural choices. Point CLAUDE.md at docs/adr and have Claude draft an ADR after any session that made a decision.
See also: Docs drift, Repository as system of record, AGENTS.md		 Custodian & automation 2026-09-03 Harness engineering: leveraging Codex in an agent-first world · agent-skills: documentation-and-adrs skill
LLM-as-judge Using a model to grade outputs against criteria; valid only after validating the judge against human labels, and Husain rejects generic judge metrics. /goal's Haiku evaluator and adversarial review subagents are LLM-as-judge inside Claude Code.
See also: Evals, Error analysis, /goal		 Evals & observability 2026-09-03 AI Evals: Everything You Need to Know (FAQ) · Demystifying evals for AI agents
Long-running agent harness Anthropic's November 2025 pattern for work spanning many context windows: an initializer agent expands the prompt into a feature list (feature_list.json), init.sh and progress file; a coding agent is woken repeatedly, reads the progress file and git log, does one feature, verifies end to end, commits, and leaves handoff notes. Key finding: summarization alone is insufficient, full resets with structured handoffs are needed.
See also: Progress file, Fresh-context loop, Definition of done, Premature completion		 Autonomy 2026-09-03 Effective harnesses for long-running agents · Long-running agents
Loop engineering George Witt's term (also used by Addy Osmani and the AI Engineer 2026 trend list) for deciding who tells an autonomous agent to stop: --max-turns, --max-budget-usd, wall-clock timeouts, concurrency locks, daily run caps, auto-pause after N consecutive failures, and model choice per job.
See also: Ralph Wiggum loop, Headless mode (claude -p)		 Custodian & automation 2026-09-03 Loop Engineering: Who Tells Claude Code and Codex When to Stop? · AIEWF 2026 trends (harness engineering, loop engineering, skills everywhere) · Manage costs effectively
Lost in the middle The 2023 finding that models attend best to the start and end of a long context and worst to the middle. It is why critical instructions go at the top of CLAUDE.md and why Anthropic says to put long documents first and the question last.
See also: Context rot		 Context management 2026-09-03 Context Rot: How Increasing Input Tokens Impacts LLM Performance · Prompting best practices
Mailbox Agent teams' file-backed inbox per teammate (~/.claude/teams/{name}/inboxes/{agent}.json) through which the lead and teammates exchange messages. Known open bugs include silent drops from background teammates and sends to misspelled names that 'succeed' into orphan inboxes, so verify with the task list, not the message.
See also: Agent teams, Cross-session messaging		 Inter-agent communication 2026-09-03 Orchestrate teams of Claude Code sessions
MAST The Multi-Agent System Failure Taxonomy from Cemri et al. (UC Berkeley, March 2025): 1,600+ traces annotated into 14 failure modes in three groups: system design (43.8%: disobeying spec, step repetition, missing termination), inter-agent misalignment (32.2%), and task verification (23.5%). The research vocabulary for what goes wrong.
See also: Inter-agent misalignment, Premature completion, Mert Cemri et al. (UC Berkeley)		 Failure modes 2026-09-03 Why Do Multi-Agent LLM Systems Fail? (MAST) · Why multi-agent LLM systems fail & how to fix them
Matt Pocock TypeScript educator turned AI Hero; his skills repo (grill-me, handoff, TDD, spec discipline) is among the most-installed opinionated Claude Code setups, and he argues for clearing rather than compacting and for deep modules with minimal interfaces.

MCP (Model Context Protocol) Anthropic's November 2024 open standard for agent-to-tool access ('USB-C for AI'), now under the Linux Foundation's Agentic AI Foundation with every major vendor supporting it and roughly half a billion SDK downloads a month. In Claude Code servers are local, project (.mcp.json) or user scoped; Anthropic advises preferring CLIs like gh when they exist.
See also: MCP supply chain risk, A2A, Agent-computer interface (tool design)		 Protocols & frameworks 2026-09-03 Model Context Protocol (GitHub org) · Connect Claude Code to tools via MCP
MCP supply chain risk Any MCP server, skill or plugin in your dependency graph can steer your agent: 2026 saw CVEs in MCP SDKs, a poisoned npm MCP package, tool-poisoning attacks via descriptions, and NSA/CISA MCP guidance. Vet servers like dependencies, pin versions, prefer first-party connectors.

Mert Cemri et al. (UC Berkeley) Lead author (with Matei Zaharia, Ion Stoica, Joseph Gonzalez and others) of 'Why Do Multi-Agent LLM Systems Fail?', the MAST taxonomy of 14 failure modes that gives multi-agent failures their names.

Message A single message from either party (Mike or Claude). The atom below Exchange in Mike's working vocabulary; in the Messages API it is one entry in the messages array with a role of user or assistant.
See also: Exchange, Turn, Session		 Working vocabulary 2026-09-03 Mike's global CLAUDE.md (Glossary section) · Using the Messages API (alternating user/assistant turns)
Meta-prompting Using an LLM to write or refine a prompt for an LLM: the formal name for Mike's dump-organize-hand-off habit. Anthropic ships a Console prompt generator (the cookbook 'metaprompt' notebook) and a prompt improver that adds structure, examples and reasoning; PromptHub's Dan Cleary catalogs the variants (APE, DSPy, TextGrad).

Model routing by role Assigning models by job: Opus or high effort for planning and review, Sonnet for implementation workers, Haiku for cheap subagents and evaluators; Boris Cherny's counterpoint is Opus for everything because less steering is faster overall. Never switch models casually mid-session (cache).
See also: Effort levels, Subagents		 Engineering practice 2026-09-03 Manage costs effectively · Pricing (Claude Platform docs) · How Boris Cherny uses Claude Code (132+ tips)
One scoped ask per turn The dictation discipline of resisting 'monologuing' five tasks into one message: one brief per task, read the diff, then the next ask, because entangled diffs cannot be reviewed with confidence. If a dump has several asks, split it into separately runnable tasks with dependencies.

OpenAI Agents SDK OpenAI's lightweight framework (March 2025, successor to Swarm) whose vocabulary many teams borrow: agents, handoffs (pass the whole conversation by default, trim with input_filter, type with input_type), guardrails, sessions and tracing.

Orchestration tax Addy Osmani's name for the coordination cost of multi-agent setups: Anthropic reports 3-10x (Research: ~15x) the tokens of a single agent plus handoff and review overhead, so multi-agent must earn its cost through context protection, parallelization or specialization.
See also: Orchestrator-workers, WIP limit, Quadratic coordination cost		 Engineering practice 2026-09-03 Addy Osmani Substack (Practical Loop Engineering, The 80% Problem) · When to use multi-agent systems (and when not to)
Orchestrator (overseer) The lead session or agent that plans, decomposes work, delegates to workers and synthesizes results without doing the implementation itself. Mike's 'overseer' session is an orchestrator; the literature also says supervisor, lead agent or manager agent.
See also: Orchestrator-workers, Worker (specialist), Task brief, Orchestrator bloat		 Working vocabulary 2026-09-03 Building Effective AI Agents · How we built our multi-agent research system · The Code Agent Orchestra: what makes multi-agent coding work
Orchestrator bloat Mike's own symptom, documented in the literature as the lead's context filling with workers' transcripts and pasted specs until it enters the dumb zone; the fix is summaries plus paths, subagents for reads, and keeping the lead out of implementation.
See also: Orchestrator (overseer), Dumb zone, Artifact-plus-pointer handoff		 Failure modes 2026-09-03 How we built our multi-agent research system · When to use multi-agent systems (and when not to) · Advanced Context Engineering for Coding Agents (Dex Horthy talk notes, 'dumb zone')
Orchestrator-workers Anthropic's name (Building Effective Agents, December 2024) for a central LLM that dynamically decomposes a task, delegates to workers and synthesizes results; recommended when subtasks cannot be predicted up front. Also called the supervisor, lead-agent or hierarchical pattern; Anthropic's Research feature is a production example (Opus lead, Sonnet subagents, ~90% better but ~15x tokens).
See also: Orchestrator (overseer), Worker (specialist), Task brief, Orchestration tax, Decompose by context boundary		 Multi-agent patterns 2026-09-03 Building Effective AI Agents · How we built our multi-agent research system · When to use multi-agent systems (and when not to)
Path-scoped rules .claude/rules/*.md files with a paths: frontmatter glob so guidance loads only when matching files are touched; the place for language- or directory-specific rules that would bloat CLAUDE.md.
See also: CLAUDE.md, Extension decision matrix		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 How Claude remembers your project (CLAUDE.md, auto memory, rules) · Set up Claude Code in a monorepo or large codebase
Permission modes Claude Code's spectrum of how much it asks: Manual (ask), acceptEdits, plan (read-only), auto (a classifier approves), dontAsk (auto-deny unapproved), and bypassPermissions (--dangerously-skip-permissions). The literal control knob for how independent an agent is allowed to be.
See also: Auto mode, Allowlist, Sandboxing, Plan mode		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 Choose a permission mode · Security (Claude Code docs)
Plan The 'how' that follows a spec: files to change, sequence, and how it will be verified, typically produced in plan mode and saved as PLAN.md or plan.md. Plans written in plan mode survive compaction, so a written plan is also a context-resilience tool.
See also: Spec, Plan mode, What survives compaction		 Working vocabulary 2026-09-03 Best practices for Claude Code · Explore the context window (What survives compaction)
Plan mode A read-only mode (Shift+Tab, or --permission-mode plan) where Claude explores and proposes a plan you approve or edit (Ctrl+G) before any edits. Use it when the approach is uncertain or many files change; skip it when you could describe the diff in one sentence. Boris Cherny starts most sessions here, though he reports needing it less with 4.6+ models.
See also: Plan, Permission modes, Interview-me (question-first method)		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 Best practices for Claude Code · Common workflows (Reference files, images, plan mode) · How Boris Cherny uses Claude Code (132+ tips)
Planner / executor (architect / editor) Splitting the model that plans from the model that edits. Aider's architect mode is the one-tool version (a planner model plus an editor model); Mike's overseer-plus-specialists layout is the multi-session version.
See also: Orchestrator-workers, Plan mode		 Multi-agent patterns 2026-09-03 Repository map | aider · How comparable tools do it (cluster A topic; Codex vs Claude Code vs Gemini CLI)
Plugins Installable bundles of skills, hooks, subagents, MCP servers and LSP servers from a marketplace (Anthropic's official one has 200+). Code-intelligence (LSP) plugins give Claude jump-to-definition and type errors after edits instead of grepping; Superpowers, Compound Engineering and ralph-loop ship as plugins.
See also: Skills (SKILL.md), Hooks, MCP (Model Context Protocol), Extension decision matrix		 Claude Code features 2026-09-03 Discover and install prebuilt plugins through marketplaces
Positive instructions State the desired behavior instead of the forbidden one ('write in flowing prose' beats 'do not use markdown'), delete earlier versions when you change your mind, and reserve emphasis for one line: current models overtrigger on 'CRITICAL: You MUST', and contradictory rules get picked arbitrarily.
See also: Golden rule of prompting, CLAUDE.md		 Prompting 2026-09-03 Prompting best practices · How Claude remembers your project (CLAUDE.md, auto memory, rules)
PR-not-commit Unattended agents land code as draft PRs on their own branch (Routines reject pushes to protected branches or branches with others' commits) and never commit to branches humans or other agents are working on; reports go to issues, code goes to PRs, never both silently.
See also: Safe outputs, Routines, Git discipline		 Custodian & automation 2026-09-03 Automate work with routines · github/gh-aw README (safe outputs)
Premature completion Workers declaring victory early or the lead accepting 'done' without checking; MAST's task-verification group (23.5%) and a key finding of Anthropic's harness work ('agents try to do everything at once and declare done too early'). Cured by an executable definition of done judged by someone other than the worker.
See also: Definition of done, MAST, Long-running agent harness		 Failure modes 2026-09-03 Why Do Multi-Agent LLM Systems Fail? (MAST) · Effective harnesses for long-running agents · When to use multi-agent systems (and when not to)
Progress file A file (claude-progress.txt, progress.md, task_plan.md) the agent updates as it works and reads first on every boot, so a fresh session can resume. Central to Anthropic's long-running-agent harness and to every Ralph-style loop.
See also: HANDOFF.md pattern, Long-running agent harness, Structured note-taking, Repository as system of record		 Inter-agent communication 2026-09-03 Effective harnesses for long-running agents · planning-with-files (repo)
Progressive disclosure Loading only names and short descriptions into context and pulling full content on demand. It is the mechanism behind skills (a ~100-token description until invoked), deferred MCP tool schemas, and Anthropic's Agent Skills standard.
See also: Skills (SKILL.md), Just-in-time context		 Context management 2026-09-03 Equipping agents for the real world with Agent Skills · Extend Claude with skills · Effective context engineering for AI agents
Prompt caching Anthropic's mechanism for re-reading a stable prefix (system prompt, tools, prior turns) at a fraction of the price within a 5-minute or 1-hour TTL. Claude Code caches automatically and reports hit rates in /usage; it is why a one-line question late in an all-day session can still cost a lot.

Prompt chaining Feeding one LLM call's output into the next as a fixed sequence (draft -> review -> refine). Anthropic's simplest workflow pattern and the formal name for Mike's dump-organize-hand-off habit; worth using explicitly when you want to inspect the intermediate artifact.
See also: Meta-prompting, Brain dump to brief, Building Effective Agents patterns		 Multi-agent patterns 2026-09-03 Building Effective AI Agents · Prompting best practices · Prompt Chaining
Prompt engineering Crafting the instructions, examples and structure of a single prompt to steer output. Anthropic now frames it as a subset of context engineering; Boris Cherny says its tricks are 'largely not important' next to giving the model the task and a way to verify.
See also: Context engineering, Meta-prompting, Golden rule of prompting		 Prompting 2026-09-03 Prompting best practices · Andrej Karpathy on X: +1 for 'context engineering' over 'prompt engineering'
Prompt injection Term coined by Simon Willison (September 2022) for attacks where content the model reads (a web page, issue, file, tool result) contains instructions it then follows. Indirect prompt injection is the agent-relevant form, and OWASP reports it still drives most agentic security failures in production; it is unsolved for fully autonomous agents.
See also: Lethal trifecta, Instruction/data boundary, Sandboxing, Simon Willison		 Security & safety 2026-09-03 Simon Willison: about (coined 'prompt injection' and 'lethal trifecta') · Prompt injection still drives most agentic AI security failures in production (OWASP) · Security (Claude Code docs)
Quadratic coordination cost Dave Paola's contrarian point that coordination overhead between parallel agents grows roughly with the square of their number, so beyond a few workers the merge and alignment cost eats the throughput gain; the argument for WIP limits and single-writer discipline.
See also: WIP limit, Orchestration tax, Single-writer principle		 Failure modes 2026-09-03 Stop parallelizing your AI agents
Ralph Wiggum loop Geoffrey Huntley's technique: while :; do cat PROMPT.md | claude ; done, re-feeding a prompt with a brand-new context each pass; the model picks the next task from specs and a fix plan, implements, tests, commits. Anthropic ships it as the ralph-wiggum plugin (Stop-hook based, with a completion promise). Needs backpressure, greenfield tolerance and senior oversight.
See also: Fresh-context loop, Backpressure, Stop hook, Geoffrey Huntley		 Autonomy 2026-09-03 Ralph Wiggum as a 'software engineer' · Ralph Wiggum plugin README
Repository as system of record OpenAI's harness-engineering rule 'if it isn't in the repo it doesn't exist': design docs, plans, progress and debt tracking are versioned so any fresh session can pick up, and the context window stops being the source of truth.

Telephone game The failure where a spec or decision degrades each time it is paraphrased across a handoff. Anthropic's January 2026 post names context loss at handoffs as a core multi-agent failure; the PerspectiveGap benchmark measured orchestration prompts omitting needed shared background 12-45% of the time. Thread / conversation / chat The individual chat thread has no single industry name. Claude Code: 'session' (with ID, --resume; docs also say 'conversation'). claude.ai: 'conversation' in help titles and 'chats' in features. Traces and spans Trace-first observability where every LLM call, tool call and subagent becomes a span you can inspect, cost and turn into an eval case. Turn One pass of Claude acting: tool calls, subagents and all, ending when Claude yields back to the human. The API sense differs: Anthropic's Messages API models 'alternating user and assistant conversational turns' where one turn is one assistant (or user) message, while Claude Code's --max-turns counts 'agentic turns', i.e. one loop pass. Mike's usage follows the agentic sense, so a turn and an exchange are not interchangeable.

Two-corrections rule Anthropic's explicit rule: after two failed corrections on the same issue, /clear and restart with a better prompt, because 'a clean session with a better prompt almost always outperforms a long session with accumulated corrections'. The docs list 'correcting over and over' as a named failure pattern.

Verification loop Anthropic's and Boris Cherny's top-ranked practice: give Claude a check it can run (tests, build, lint, screenshot diff, script) so it iterates until pass instead of stopping when work looks done; Cherny claims 2-3x quality. The escalation ladder is in-prompt check -> /goal -> Stop hook -> verification subagent, and 'show evidence, not assertions' is the reporting rule.

Current content (18 lines)
# Response style (Mike's preferences — apply in every session)

- Keep a relatively casual, lighthearted tone with a good amount of humor woven in, while
  still giving substantive, honest answers.
- Keep responses concise and non-repetitive. Don't restate the same point across multiple
  sections or meander.
- When an answer is necessarily long, end it with a short **Bottom line** summarizing the key points.

# Glossary (shared vocabulary — use these terms consistently)

- **Message** — a single message from either party. The atom below "exchange".
- **Exchange** — one message from Mike, one response from Claude. Deliberately not a formal
  term of art, so it is unambiguous. One exchange may contain many turns on Claude's side.
- **Turn** — one pass of Claude acting: tool calls, sub-agents, and all. Multiple turns can
  happen inside a single exchange, so "turn" and "exchange" are not interchangeable.
- **Session** — one Claude Code conversation thread with an ID, resumable via `--resume`.
- **Fork** — a branch off an existing session that inherits its context. Used constantly.

Current content (27 lines)
# Hub: central_dev_management_and_improvements

This directory is Mike's hub for work that spans all his Claude Code projects. Deliverables live on the private
website page (Growth and Best Practices) and are generated from JSON in `research/` by `tools/build_growth_page.py`.

## Rules for every exchange in this directory

- **Log the exchange.** Before finishing a response, append two rows to `research/exchange-log.json` (one for Mike's
  ask, one for Claude's response), each a one- or two-sentence summary. Then rebuild and push the page. Never rewrite
  earlier rows.
- **Directory first.** Any new topic or domain Mike mentions gets a row in `research/registry.json` before any
  research starts, with `status: unresearched` and an empty `research_log`. Research passes append a `research_log`
  entry with date, what was done, agent count, search count, and output. Depth is computed by the build script from
  evidence; never hand-edit it.
- **Only Mike verifies.** `review_log` entries are added only when Mike says he reviewed something. The
  work-in-progress banner stays on the learning plan until the first review entry exists.
- **Action items.** Anything Mike asks for that cannot or should not be finished in the current exchange goes into
  `research/action-items.json` with a reason. Mark items `done` rather than deleting them.
- **Rebuild command:** `python tools/build_growth_page.py` (add `--summary out.html` for the Drive index doc).
  Then commit and push in `C:\dev\michaelfitzpatrick-website`.

## Gotchas

- The website repo uses CRLF; the build script preserves line endings. Large bash heredocs fail in this shell; use
  the Write tool for new files.
- The page is obscurity-only (no auth). Nothing sensitive goes on it.

# Otto — Project Memory

Otto is Mike's personal life-management app: habit tracking, action items, daily briefings, journaling, and goals. Pre-code phase; we're building the plan and portal first.

## Tone & vibe (project setting #1, set 2026-09-02)
- Be casual, fun, jovial, joking, and optimistic — the engineer Mike would grab a beer with.
- Tone never compromises quality: when doing research, architecture, code, or debugging, deliver rigorous, best-in-class work. Jokes go in the margins, not the load-bearing walls.
- Mike has ADHD; celebrate momentum, keep things fun, help him stay on target when he's supposed to be doing something else.

## Project facts
- Working dir: C:\dev\Otto (future app code). Not yet a git repo.
- Web portal ("Otto HQ") source: C:\dev\michaelfitzpatrick-website\otto\index.html — canonical copy lives there, NOT in this repo.
- Portal URL (temporary): http://michaelfitzpatrick.me/otto — deploys via commit+push to master of michaelfitzpatrick-website (GitHub → Render auto-deploy, ~1-2 min).
- Long-term hosting target for the app itself: Google Cloud Platform.
- Detailed project context (priorities, seed habits, naming decisions) lives in auto-memory — check MEMORY.md.

## Conventions
- Session naming: sessions identify themselves in the portal's Researcher/Owner columns; this founding session is "HQ".
- Portal tables use thin collapsed rows (<details>) — preserve that pattern when adding records.
- Section names are provisional and playful (The Glovebox, The Stacks, The Grand Tour); alternatives are tracked in yellow boxes on the page — don't rename without Mike.
- When Mike asks for research, always record the Origin (date + why) in The Stacks so nobody forgets why it happened.
